\displaystyle=\ \delta_{i}\hbox{log}\biggr\{\int_{w_{i}\leq C}f_{C|{\mathbf{Z}}}(c,{\mathbf{z}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{\eta}})f_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}(w_{i},{\mathbf{z}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{\gamma}})dc\biggr\}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+(1-\delta_{i})\hbox{log}\biggr\{\int_{w_{i}\leq X}f_{C|{\mathbf{Z}}}(w_{i},{\mathbf{z}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{\eta}})f_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}(x_{i},{\mathbf{z}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{\gamma}})dx\biggr\}.

The second equality follows since
X\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}C|{\mathbf{Z}}
. The corresponding score equation equals

\displaystyle{\mathbf{S}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}(w_{i},\delta_{i},{\mathbf{z}}_{i};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}}\hbox{log}f_{W,\Delta|{\mathbf{Z}}}(w_{i},\delta_{i},{\mathbf{z}}_{i};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{i}\frac{\int_{w_{i}\leq C}\{\partial\hbox{log}f_{C,X|{\mathbf{Z}}}(c,w_{i},{\mathbf{z}}_{i};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$})/\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}\}f_{C,X|{\mathbf{Z}}}(c,w_{i},{\mathbf{z}}_{i};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$})dc}{\int_{w_{i}\leq C}f_{C,X|{\mathbf{Z}}}(c,w_{i},{\mathbf{z}}_{i};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$})dc}

\displaystyle+(1-\delta_{i})\frac{\int_{w_{i}\leq X}\{\partial\hbox{log}f_{C,X|{\mathbf{Z}}}(w_{i},x,{\mathbf{z}}_{i};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$})/\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}\}f_{C,X|{\mathbf{Z}}}(w_{i},x,{\mathbf{z}}_{i};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$})dx}{\int_{w_{i}\leq X}f_{C,X|{\mathbf{Z}}}(w_{i},x,{\mathbf{z}}_{i};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$})dx}.

Finally, let
{\mathbf{O}}^{*}=(W,\Delta,{\mathbf{Z}})
and
\widehat{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}
be the solution to the estimating equation

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*}_{i};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbf{S}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}(w_{i},\delta_{i},{\mathbf{z}}_{i};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$})={\mathbf{0}}.

(S.1)

S.2.2

Consistency

Each estimator we consider is a solution to so-called
m
-estimating equations. Specifically, the estimator
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}
is the solution to
\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm est}({\mathbf{O}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$})={\mathbf{0}}
, where
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm est}(\cdot)
is an estimating function evaluated at observed data
{\mathbf{O}}_{i}
, and
\alpha
represents the set of nuisance parameters. For the case of the CC estimator, we consider
\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm est}({\mathbf{O}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})={\mathbf{0}}
, as it does not depend on any nuisance parameters. We use the following regularity conditions when proving the consistency and asymptotic normality of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
(Section
S.2.4
):

(A1)

{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}\in\mbox{\boldmath$\Omega$}
, and
\Omega
is compact.

(A2)

E[\sup_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\in\Omega}\|{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm est}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$})\|_{2}]<\infty
.

(A3)

E\{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm est}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})/\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}\}
has bounded eigenvalues and is invertible.

(A4)

E\{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm est}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})/\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}\}
has bounded eigenvalues for
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\in\mbox{\boldmath$\Omega$}
.

The first two conditions,
(A1)
and
(A2)
, are conditions that show consistency of the estimator and are typically assumed in large sample theory
(
Newey & McFadden, 1994a
)
. Condition
(A3)
assumes that the solution of the
m
-estimating equation is well-behaved and unique, two other standard conditions under large sample theory. Finally, condition
(A4)
regulates the influence of the nuisance parameters by assuming finite eigenvalues. This assumption is reasonable since we consider a finite-dimensional nuisance parameter set, and in our implementation, we consider a parametric model for estimating
\alpha
that ensures that the function is sufficiently smooth. Additionally, we assume that all probabilities are bounded (not including) 0 and 1. For the case of the CC estimator, condition
(A4)
is not necessary since the
m
-estimating equation does not depend on
\alpha
. Throughout, we work to prove that
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
is a consistent estimator of
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
by showing its estimating equation is unbiased; i.e.,
E\{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm est}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})\}={\mathbf{0}}
. By showing that the estimator is unbiased and assuming standard regularity conditions
(A1)
-
(A4)
, the estimator
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
is consistent and asymptotically normal by following Theorem 2.6 of
Newey & McFadden (1994a)
.

1.

Nuisance distribution estimators:

Since
\int f_{W,\Delta|{\mathbf{Z}}}(w,\delta,{\mathbf{z}};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})dwd\delta=1
, it follows that

\displaystyle{\mathbf{0}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}}\int f_{W,\Delta|{\mathbf{Z}}}(w,\delta,{\mathbf{z}};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})dwd\delta

(S.2)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\biggr\{\frac{\partial}{\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}}\hbox{log}f_{W,\Delta|{\mathbf{Z}}}(w,\delta,{\mathbf{z}};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})\biggr\}f_{W,\Delta|{\mathbf{Z}}}(w,\delta,{\mathbf{z}};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})dwd\delta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int{\mathbf{S}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}(w,\delta,{\mathbf{z}};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})f_{W,\Delta|{\mathbf{Z}}}(w,\delta,{\mathbf{z}};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})dwd\delta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{W,\Delta|{\mathbf{Z}}}\{{\mathbf{S}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}(W,\Delta,{\mathbf{Z}};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})\}.

The second equality holds by standard regularity conditions that allow us to interchange the order of the integral and partial derivative. It therefore follows that

\displaystyle E_{W,\Delta,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{{\mathbf{S}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}(W,\Delta,{\mathbf{Z}};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{{\mathbf{Z}}}[E_{W,\Delta|{\mathbf{Z}}}\{{\mathbf{S}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}(W,\Delta,{\mathbf{Z}};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})\}]={\mathbf{0}}.

2.

CC estimator:

Note that
E_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\}={\mathbf{0}}
following a similar argument as in equation (
S.2
), where
{\mathbf{S}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}
is replaced by
{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}
. Then,

\displaystyle E\left\{\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}[E_{W,\Delta|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left\{\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right\}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}[E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left\{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0}){\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right\}+E_{C|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{0}})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}[\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})E_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\}]={\mathbf{0}}.

3.

IPW estimator:

\displaystyle E\left\{\frac{\delta{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\biggr[E_{W,\Delta|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left\{\frac{\delta{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}\right\}\biggr]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\biggr[E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left\{\frac{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}\times{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right\}+E_{C|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{0}})\biggr]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left\{{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right\}={\mathbf{0}}.

4.

MLE:

\displaystyle E\{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm{MLE}}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{0})\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\left\{{\delta{\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})}\right\}

\displaystyle+E\left\{\left(1-{\delta}\right)\frac{\int_{c<X}{\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}(y,x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})f_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})f_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}(x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{0})dx}{\int_{c<X}f_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})f_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}(x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{0})dx}\right\}.

The first term has a mean of zero, as it corresponds to the estimating equation for the CC estimator. The second term also has a mean of zero, as shown by first noting it is equivalent to the following:

\displaystyle\int_{Y,W,\Delta,{\mathbf{Z}}}\biggr\{\left(1-{\delta}\right)\frac{\int_{c<X}{\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}(y,x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})f_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})f_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}(x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{0})dx}{\int_{c<X}f_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})f_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}(x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{0})dx}f_{Y,W,\Delta,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,w,\delta,{\mathbf{z}})\biggr\}dydwd\delta d{\mathbf{z}}.

Using the assumptions that
C\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}
, it follows that

\displaystyle f_{Y,W,\Delta,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,w,\delta,{\mathbf{z}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\biggr\{\int_{x\leq C}f_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,w,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})f_{C,X|{\mathbf{Z}}}(c,w,{\mathbf{z}};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})dc\biggr\}^{\delta}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\biggr\{\int_{c<X}f_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})f_{C,X|{\mathbf{Z}}}(w,x,{\mathbf{z}};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})dx\biggr\}^{1-\delta}.

When
\delta=1
, the expectation is
{\mathbf{0}}
. When
\delta=0
the expectation is equal to

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{Y,C,{\mathbf{Z}}}\biggr\{\frac{\int_{c\leq X}{\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}(y,x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})f_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})dx}{\int_{c\leq X}f_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})f_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}(x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{0})dx}\biggr\}

\displaystyle\quad\times\biggr\{\int_{c\leq X}f_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})f_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}(x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{0})dx\biggr\}f_{C|{\mathbf{Z}}}(c,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})f_{\mathbf{Z}}({\mathbf{z}})dydcd{\mathbf{z}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{Y,C,{\mathbf{Z}}}\int_{c\leq X}{\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}(y,x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})f_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})f_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}(x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{0})dxf_{C|{\mathbf{Z}}}(c,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})f_{\mathbf{Z}}({\mathbf{z}})dydcd{\mathbf{z}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{C,{\mathbf{Z}}}\int_{c\leq X}\biggr\{\int_{Y}{\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}(y,x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})f_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})dy\biggr\}f_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}(x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{0})dxf_{C,{\mathbf{Z}}}(c,{\mathbf{z}})dcd{\mathbf{z}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{C,{\mathbf{Z}}}\int_{c\leq X}{\mathbf{0}}\times f_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}(x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\gamma}})dxf_{C|{\mathbf{Z}}}(c,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})f_{\mathbf{Z}}({\mathbf{z}})dcd{\mathbf{z}}={\mathbf{0}}.

The last line follows since
\int{\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}(y,x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})f_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})dy=E_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{{\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}(Y,x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\}={\mathbf{0}}
. Therefore,
E\{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm{MLE}}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{0})\}={\mathbf{0}}
.

5.

ACC estimator:
It suffices to show that the expectation of the augmentation part of the ACC estimator is zero since we already showed its first part, the estimating equation for the CC estimator, has mean zero. Observe that

\displaystyle E_{Y,W,\Delta,{\mathbf{Z}}}[\{\Delta-\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})]

\displaystyle=E_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}[E_{W,\Delta|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{\Delta-\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})].

Consistency is achieved if
E_{W,\Delta|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{\Delta-\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})\}={\mathbf{0}}
. That result follows since

\displaystyle E_{W,\Delta|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{\Delta-\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{W,\Delta|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(\Delta)-\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})-\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})={\mathbf{0}}.

Since
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC}
is a non-random matrix, the proof of consistency stays the same when modifying the ACC estimator to include
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC}
.

6.

MACC estimator:

Like the ACC estimator, it suffices to show that the augmentation part of the MACC estimator has mean zero. Observe that

\displaystyle E_{Y,W,\Delta,{\mathbf{Z}}}[\{1-\Delta/\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm MACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})]

\displaystyle=E_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}[E_{W,\Delta|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{1-\Delta/\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm MACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})],

where we achieve consistency if
E_{W,\Delta|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{1-\Delta/\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})\}=0
. Using rules of conditional expectation, it follows that

\displaystyle E_{W,\Delta|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{1-\delta/\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-E_{W,\Delta|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{\Delta/\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})/\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-1=0.

Since
E_{W,\Delta|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{1-\Delta/\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})\}=0
, then it follows that
E_{Y,W,\Delta,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm MACC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})\}={\mathbf{0}}
.

Since
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}
is assumed to be a non-random matrix, the same consistency properties apply when the MACC estimator uses
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}
.

7.

AIPW estimator:

The estimating equation for the AIPW estimator combines the IPW estimating equations with an augmentation part. We already showed that the IPW estimating equations have mean zero. To prove consistency, it remains to be shown that the augmentation part of the AIPW estimating equations also has a mean of zero. In that augmentation part,
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
is any function of
(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
. By interchanging the roles of
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
with
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm MACC}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
, the proof of consistency and robustness is the same as that for the MACC estimator.

Since
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm AIPW}
is a non-random matrix, the same consistency properties hold when the AIPW estimator uses
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm AIPW}
.

S.2.3

Robustness

We explain when each estimator is robust to the misspecification of the nuisance distributions.

1.

CC estimator:

The CC estimator is robust to the misspecification of
f_{C|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}
,
f_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}
, and
f_{{\mathbf{Z}}}
, as these nuisance distributions are not used in estimating
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
.

2.

IPW estimator:

Consider the case that
f_{C|{\mathbf{Z}}}
is incorrectly specified as
f_{C|{\mathbf{Z}}}^{*}
. Let
\pi^{*}_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}})=\int_{x\leq C}f^{*}_{C|{\mathbf{Z}}}(c,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}})dc
. Then,

\displaystyle E\left\{\frac{\delta{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})}{\pi^{*}_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}})}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\biggr[E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left\{\frac{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}{\pi^{*}_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}})}\times{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right\}+E_{C|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{0}})\biggr]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}\biggr[\frac{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}{\pi^{*}_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}})}\times E_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left\{{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right\}\biggr]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}\biggr[\frac{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}{\pi^{*}_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}})}\times{\mathbf{0}}\biggr]={\mathbf{0}}.

The last line follows since
E_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left\{{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right\}={\mathbf{0}}
. Therefore, the IPW estimator is robust to the misspecification of
f_{C|{\mathbf{Z}}}
,
f_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}
, and
f_{{\mathbf{Z}}}
.

Now consider the case when the probability of being observed is defined as
\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})\equiv\hbox{pr}(\Delta=1|Y=y,{\mathbf{Z}}={\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})
. Then,

\displaystyle E\left\{\frac{\delta{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})}{\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\biggr[E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left\{\frac{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}{\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})}\times{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right\}+E_{C|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{0}})\biggr]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}\biggr[\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})\times E_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left\{\frac{{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})}{\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})}\right\}\biggr]

\displaystyle\neq

\displaystyle E_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left\{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})\times{\mathbf{0}}\right\}.

Since
E_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left\{{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})/\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(Y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})\right\}\neq{\mathbf{0}}
, using the probability
\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})
will result in an inconsistent estimator of
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
for our setting.

3.

MLE:

Suppose we misspecify the nuisance distribution
f_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}(x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\gamma}})
as
f_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}^{*}(x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\gamma}})
. The part corresponding to the CC estimator remains consistent, but the second part is now

\displaystyle\int_{Y,C,{\mathbf{Z}}}\biggr\{\frac{\int_{c<X}{\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}(y,x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})f_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})f^{*}_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}(x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\gamma}})dx}{\int_{c<X}f_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})f^{*}_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}(x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\gamma}})dx}\biggr\}

\displaystyle\quad\times\biggr\{\int_{c\leq X}f_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})f_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}(x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\gamma}})dx\biggr\}f_{C,{\mathbf{Z}}}(c,{\mathbf{z}})dydcd{\mathbf{z}}

\displaystyle\neq\int_{Y,C,{\mathbf{Z}}}\int_{c<X}{\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}(y,x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})f_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})f^{*}_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}(x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\gamma}})f_{C,{\mathbf{Z}}}(c,{\mathbf{z}})dxdydcd{\mathbf{z}}.

In this case, the integral in the denominator on the first line would no longer cancel with the integral with respect to
X
on the second line. Therefore, the conditional integral with respect to
Y
is not equal to
{\mathbf{0}}
. There is no way to generally simplify this expression as is. Therefore, the MLE is not robust to the misspecification of
f_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}
, but it is robust to the misspecification of
f_{C|{\mathbf{Z}}}
and
f_{{\mathbf{Z}}}
.

4.

ACC estimator:

To show that the ACC estimator is only singly robust, it suffices to show that the estimating equation is unbiased only when the probabilities are correctly specified. We have already proved that the ACC estimator is consistent when the probabilities are correctly specified. Now, consider the case that
\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}})
is incorrectly specified as
\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}^{*}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})
and
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})={\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}^{\rm eff}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
, where
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}^{\rm eff}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})=-E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}},\Delta=1}\{{\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}(y,X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\}
. We later show that this choice guarantees higher efficiency over the CC estimator. Then, it follows

\displaystyle E_{Y,W,\Delta,{\mathbf{Z}}}[\{\Delta-\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}^{*}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}})\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})]

\displaystyle=E_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}[\{E_{W,\Delta|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(\Delta)-\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}^{*}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}})\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})]

\displaystyle=-E_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}[\{\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})-\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}^{*}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}})\}E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}},\Delta=1}\{{\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\}]

\displaystyle=-E_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}[E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}},\Delta=1}\{\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0}){\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\}]

\displaystyle\quad\quad+E_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}[E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}},\Delta=1}\{\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}^{*}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}){\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\}]

\displaystyle\neq{\mathbf{0}},

where the last line follows since
\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})\neq\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}^{*}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}})
. This result shows that the ACC estimator is consistent only when
\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})\equiv f_{\Delta|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(\delta=1,y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})
is correctly specified. Therefore, the ACC estimator is not robust to the misspecification of
f_{\Delta|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}
but is robust to the misspecification of
f_{C,X|{\mathbf{Z}}}
and
f_{{\mathbf{Z}}}
.

Since
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC}
is a non-random matrix, the same robustness properties apply as with the ACC estimator without
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC}
.

5.

MACC estimator:

To show that the MACC estimator is only singly robust, it suffices to show that the estimating equation is unbiased only when the probabilities are correctly specified. We have already proved that the MACC estimator is consistent when the probabilities are correctly specified. Now, consider the case when
f_{C|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}
is incorrectly specified as
f_{C|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}^{*}
and
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm MACC}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})={\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm MACC}^{\rm eff}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
, where
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}^{\rm eff}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})=E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}[\{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})-1\}{\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})]/E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{1-1/\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})\}
. We later show that this choice guarantees higher efficiency over the CC estimator. Let
\pi^{*}_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}})=\int_{x\leq C}f^{*}_{C|{\mathbf{Z}}}(c,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{*})dc
. Then,

\displaystyle E_{Y,W,\Delta,{\mathbf{Z}}}[\{1-\Delta/\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}^{*}(X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}})\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}^{\rm eff}_{\rm MACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})]

\displaystyle=E_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}([1-E_{W,\Delta|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{\Delta/\pi^{*}_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}})\}]{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}^{\rm eff}_{\rm MACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}))

\displaystyle=E_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}([1-E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})/\pi^{*}_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}})\}]{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}^{\rm eff}_{\rm MACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}))

\displaystyle=E_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left(\left[1-E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left\{\frac{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}^{*}(X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}})}\right\}\right]\frac{E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}[\{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})-1\}{\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})]}{E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{1-1/\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})\}}\right)

\displaystyle\neq{\mathbf{0}},

where the last line follows since
E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})/\pi^{*}_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}})\}\neq 1
. Therefore, the MACC estimator is only consistent when
f_{C|{\mathbf{Z}}}
is correctly specified, but is robust to misspecification to
f_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}
and
f_{{\mathbf{Z}}}
.

Since
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}
is assumed to be a non-random matrix, the same robustness properties apply as in the regular MACC.

6.

AIPW estimator:

The AIPW estimator has the same robustness as the MACC estimator. The augmented components
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
and
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm MACC}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
can be interchanged as these vectors can be any vector that is a function of
(Y,{\mathbf{Z}})
and of the same length as
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
. Therefore, the AIPW estimator is only consistent when
f_{C|{\mathbf{Z}}}
is correctly specified but is robust to the misspecification to
f_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}
and
f_{{\mathbf{Z}}}
.

When we use
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm AIPW}
in the AIPW estimator, the robustness stays the same since
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm AIPW}
remains a fixed matrix like
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}
.

S.2.4

Asymptotic Normality

We use the theory of
m
-estimating equations to prove asymptotic normality.

Only unknown parameter is
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
:

Since
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
is the solution to
\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm est}({\mathbf{O}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})={\mathbf{0}}
and we proved that
E\{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm est}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})\}={\mathbf{0}}
for all
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
, then we can perform a Taylor series expansion about the true value of
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
, denoted by
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}
:

\displaystyle{\mathbf{0}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm est}({\mathbf{O}}_{i};\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm est}({\mathbf{O}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})+n^{-1}\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm est}({\mathbf{O}}_{i};\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}})}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}}\right\}n^{1/2}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm est}({\mathbf{O}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})+E\left\{\frac{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm est}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}}\right\}n^{1/2}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})+o_{p}(1),

where
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
is an intermediate value between
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
and
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}
and
o_{p}(1)
converges to zero in probability. The last equality follows by the Law of Large Numbers. Under regularity condition
(A3)
, we have

\displaystyle n^{1/2}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left[E\left\{\frac{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm est}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}}\right\}\right]^{-1}n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm est}({\mathbf{O}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})+o_{p}(1).

This result, combined with the Central Limit Theorem and Law of Large Numbers, implies that

\displaystyle n^{1/2}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\rightarrow_{d}\hbox{Normal}({\mathbf{0}},{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}{\mathbf{B}}{\mathbf{A}}^{-T}),

where
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm est}=E\biggr\{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm est}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\biggr\}
and
{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm est}=E\biggr\{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm est}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{\otimes 2}\biggr\}
. The corresponding
i^{th}
influence function of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
is
{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\rm est}({\mathbf{O}}_{i})=-{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm est}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm est}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
, which is estimated by substituting
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
for
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}
. The sandwich estimator for the asymptotic variance of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
is
\hbox{var}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}})=\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm est}\widehat{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm est}\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm est}^{-T}
, where
\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm est}
and
\widehat{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm est}
are
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm est}
and
{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm est}
evaluated at
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
, respectively.

Additional unknown parameters besides
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
:
Suppose we have additional unknown parameters
\alpha
so that our
m
-estimating equation is of the form
\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm est}({\mathbf{O}}^{*}_{i};{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$})={\mathbf{0}}
, where
{\mathbf{O}}^{*}=(W,\Delta,{\mathbf{Z}})
. Suppose also that
\widehat{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}
is the solution to
\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*}_{i};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$})={\mathbf{0}}
. Using a first order Taylor series expansion, we have that

\displaystyle n^{1/2}(\widehat{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}-\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})=-\left\{n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*}_{i};\widetilde{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}})}{\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}}\right\}^{-1}n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*}_{i};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0}),

(S.3)

where
\widetilde{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}
is an intermediate value between
\widehat{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}
and
\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0}
. Now, consider the first order Taylor expansion of
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm est}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$})
about
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}
and
\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0}
:

\displaystyle{\mathbf{0}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm est}({\mathbf{O}}_{i};\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\rm est;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}},\widehat{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm est}({\mathbf{O}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})+n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm est}({\mathbf{O}}_{i};\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\widetilde{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}})}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}}n^{1/2}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\rm est;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})

\displaystyle+n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm est}({\mathbf{O}}_{i};\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\widetilde{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}})}{\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}}n^{1/2}(\widehat{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}-\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0}),

where
\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
is an intermediate value between
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\rm est;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}
and
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}
. Plugging in equation (
S.3
), we have

\displaystyle{\mathbf{0}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm est}({\mathbf{O}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})+n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm est}({\mathbf{O}}_{i};\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\widetilde{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}})}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}}n^{1/2}(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{\rm est;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})

\displaystyle-n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm est}({\mathbf{O}}_{i};\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\widetilde{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}})}{\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}}\left\{n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*}_{i};\widetilde{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}})}{\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}}\right\}^{-1}n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*}_{i};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})+o_{p}(1).

Assuming regularity condition
(A3)
for
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm est}({\mathbf{O}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$})
, we have

\displaystyle n^{1/2}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\rm est;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left\{n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm est}({\mathbf{O}}_{i};\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\widetilde{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}})}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}}\right\}^{-1}\Bigg[n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm est}({\mathbf{O}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})

\displaystyle-n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm est}({\mathbf{O}}_{i};\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\widetilde{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}})}{\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}}\left\{n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*}_{i};\widetilde{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}})}{\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}}\right\}^{-1}

\displaystyle\times n^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*}_{i};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})\Bigg]+o_{p}(1).

Further, using the Law of Large Numbers, the Central Limit Theorem, and Slutsky’s Theorem, it follows that

\displaystyle n^{1/2}(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{{\rm est;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\rightarrow_{d}\hbox{Normal}({\mathbf{0}},{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm est;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{-1}{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm est;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm est;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{-T}),

where
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm est;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}=E\{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm est}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})/\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{{}^{\rm T}}\}
and

\displaystyle{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm est;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\{({\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm est}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})

\displaystyle-E\{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm est}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})/\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}\}[E\{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})/\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}\}]^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0}))^{\otimes 2}\}.

The corresponding
i^{th}
influence function of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\rm est;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}
is

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\rm est;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}_{i})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm est;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{-1}\{({\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm est}({\mathbf{O}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})

\displaystyle\quad\quad-E\{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm est}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})/\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}\}[E\{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})/\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}\}]^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*}_{i};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0}))\},

and the estimate of the sandwich estimator for the asymptotic variance of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\rm est;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}
, under the presence of
\widehat{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}
, is
\hbox{var}(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\rm est;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}})\equiv\hbox{var}\{{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\rm est;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}})\}=\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm est;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{-1}\widehat{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm est;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm est;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{-T}
, where
\widehat{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm est;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}
and
\widehat{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm est;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}
are
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm est;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}
and
{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm est;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}
evaluated at
(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\rm est;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}},\widehat{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}})
, respectively. We apply these results to show that the following estimators are asymptotically normally distributed.

1.

Nuisance distribution estimators:

Estimators of the nuisance distribution parameters are asymptotically normal:

\displaystyle n^{1/2}(\widehat{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}-\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})\rightarrow_{d}\hbox{Normal}({\mathbf{0}},{\mathbf{A}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{-1}{\mathbf{B}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}{\mathbf{A}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{-T}),

where
{\mathbf{A}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}=E_{W,\Delta,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left\{\frac{\partial}{\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})\right\}
and
{\mathbf{B}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}=E_{W,\Delta,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left\{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})^{\otimes 2}\right\}
.

2.

CC estimator:

The CC estimator,
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\rm CC}
, is asymptotically normally distributed:

\displaystyle n^{1/2}(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{\rm CC}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\rightarrow_{d}\hbox{Normal}\{{\mathbf{0}},{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}^{-1}{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm CC}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}^{-T}\},

where
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}=E\left\{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right\}
and
{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm CC}=E\{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{\otimes 2}\}
.

3.

IPW estimator:

The IPW estimator,
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\rm IPW}
, is asymptotically normally distributed. When the nuisance distribution
f_{C|{\mathbf{Z}}}
is known, we have that

\displaystyle n^{1/2}(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{{\rm IPW}}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\rightarrow\hbox{Normal}({\mathbf{0}},{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}^{-1}{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm IPW}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}^{-T}),

where
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}=E\left\{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm IPW}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})\right\}
and
{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm IPW}=E\{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm IPW}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})^{\otimes 2}\}
.

When
f_{C|{\mathbf{Z}}}
is unknown, we propose a parametric nuisance distribution
f_{C|{\mathbf{Z}}}(c,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}})
and estimate
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}
via estimating
\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}=({\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{T},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{T},)^{T}
, as in Section
S.2.1
.
Adjusting for the added variability contributed by the unknown
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}
through
\alpha
, we have that

\displaystyle n^{1/2}(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{{\rm IPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\rightarrow_{d}\hbox{Normal}({\mathbf{0}},{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{-1}{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm IPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{-T}),

where
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}=E\{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm IPW}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})/\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{{}^{\rm T}}\}
and

{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm IPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}=E\{({\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm IPW}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})-E\{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm IPW}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})/\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}\}[E\{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$})/\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}\}]^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}))^{\otimes 2}\}
.

4.

MLE:

The MLE,
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\rm MLE}
, is asymptotically normally distributed. When the nuisance distribution
f_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}
is known, we have that

\displaystyle n^{1/2}(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{{\rm MLE}}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\rightarrow\hbox{Normal}\{{\mathbf{0}},{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm MLE}^{-1}{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm MLE}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm MLE}^{-T}\}.

The components of the asymptotic variance are

{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm MLE}=E\biggr\{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm MLE}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{0})\biggr\}
and

{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm MLE}=E\{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm MLE}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{0})^{\otimes 2}\}
.

When
f_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}
is unknown, we propose a parametric nuisance distribution
f_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}(c,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\gamma}})
and estimate
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
via estimating
\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}=({\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{T},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{T},)^{T}
, as in Section
S.2.1
.
Adjusting for the added variability contributed by the unknown
{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
through
\alpha
, we have that

\displaystyle n^{1/2}(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{{\rm MLE;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\rightarrow_{d}\hbox{Normal}({\mathbf{0}},{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm MLE;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{-1}{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm MLE;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm MLE;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{-T}),

where
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm MLE;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}=E\{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm MLE}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})/\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{{}^{\rm T}}\}
and

{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm MLE;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}=E\{({\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm MLE}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})-E\{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm MLE}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})/\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}\}[E\{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})/\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}\}]^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0}))^{\otimes 2}\}
.

5.

ACC estimator:

The ACC estimator,
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\rm ACC}
, is asymptotically normally distributed. Suppose that
f_{\Delta|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}
is known
and specified as
f_{\Delta|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(\delta,y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}})
.
When we do not use
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC}
, we have that

\displaystyle n^{1/2}(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{{\rm ACC}}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\rightarrow_{d}\hbox{Normal}({\mathbf{0}},{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm ACC}^{-1}{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm ACC}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm ACC}^{-T}),

where
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm ACC}=E\biggr\{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm ACC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})\biggr\}
and
{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm ACC}=E\{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm ACC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})^{\otimes 2}\}
. When using
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC}
,
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm ACC}
and
{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm ACC}
become
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}
and
{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}
, respectively. The only difference is that
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm ACC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})
is replaced by
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})
.

Moreover, we can simplify
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm ACC}
and
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}
since

\displaystyle{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm ACC}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\biggr\{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm ACC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})\biggr\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\biggr[\Delta\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}}{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})+\left\{\Delta-\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})\right\}\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\biggr]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\biggr[\Delta\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}}{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\biggr]\ {\rm since\ the\ second\ part\ equals\ {\mathbf{0}}}

and

\displaystyle{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\biggr\{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})\biggr\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\biggr[\Delta\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}}{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})+\left\{\delta-\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})\right\}{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC}\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\biggr]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\biggr[\Delta\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}}{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\biggr]\ {\rm since\ the\ second\ part\ equals\ {\mathbf{0}}.}

Therefore,
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm ACC}={\mathbf{A}}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}={\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}
, where
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}
is from the asymptotic distribution of the CC estimator.

Now suppose
f_{\Delta|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}
is unknown, and we propose a parametric nuisance distribution
f_{\Delta|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(\delta,y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}})
and estimate
{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}
using a logistic regression model with a logit-link function, as described in
Bartlett et al. (2014a)
. We have that

\displaystyle n^{1/2}(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}}}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\rightarrow_{d}\hbox{Normal}({\mathbf{0}},{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}}^{-1}{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}}^{-T}),

where
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}}=E\{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm ACC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})/\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{{}^{\rm T}}\}
and

{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}}=E\{({\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm ACC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})-E\{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm ACC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})/\partial{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}^{T}\}[E\{\partial{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})/\partial{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}^{T}\}]^{-1}{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0}))^{\otimes 2}\}
. The term
{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})
reflects the
m
-estimating equation with respect to the logistic regression model as a function of
(Y,{\mathbf{Z}})
and indexed by
{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}
.
When we do use
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC}
, we have that
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}}
and
{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}}
become

{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\kappa}},{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}
and

{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\kappa}},{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}
, respectively. The only change is that
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm ACC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})
is replaced by
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})
. Similarly, it still follows that
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}}={\mathbf{A}}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\kappa}},{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}={\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}
.

6.

MACC estimator:

The MACC estimator,
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\rm MACC}
, is asymptotically normally distributed. Suppose that
f_{C|{\mathbf{Z}}}
is known and specified as
f_{C|{\mathbf{Z}}}(c,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})
. When we do not use
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}
,

\displaystyle n^{1/2}(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{{\rm MACC}}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\rightarrow_{d}\hbox{Normal}\{{\mathbf{0}},{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm MACC}^{-1}{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm MACC}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm MACC}^{-T}\}.

The components of the asymptotic variance are
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm MACC}=E\{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm MACC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})/\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}\}
and
{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm MACC}=E\{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm MACC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})^{\otimes 2}\}
. When we do use
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}
,
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm MACC}
and
{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm MACC}
become
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm MACC;{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}
and
{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm MACC;{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}
, respectively. The only change is that
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm MACC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})
is replaced by
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm MACC;{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})
.

Moreover,
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm MACC}={\mathbf{A}}_{\rm MACC;{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}={\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}
since

\displaystyle{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm MACC}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\biggr\{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm MACC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})\biggr\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\biggr[\Delta\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}}{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})+\left\{1-\frac{\Delta}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}\right\}\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm MACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\biggr]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\biggr[\Delta\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}}{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\biggr]\ {\rm since\ the\ second\ part\ equals\ {\mathbf{0}}}

and

\displaystyle{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm MACC;{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\biggr\{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm MACC;{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})\biggr\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\biggr[\Delta\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}}{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})+\biggr\{1-\frac{\Delta}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}\biggr\}{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm MACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\biggr]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\biggr[\Delta\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}}{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\biggr]\ {\rm since\ the\ second\ part\ equals\ {\mathbf{0}}.}

Now suppose the nuisance distribution parameters
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}
are unknown in
f_{C|{\mathbf{Z}}}(c,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})
, and they are estimated by estimating
\alpha
, as in Section
S.2.1
. Then, when we do not use
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}
,

\displaystyle n^{1/2}(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{{\rm MACC;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\rightarrow_{d}\hbox{Normal}\{{\mathbf{0}},{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm MACC;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{-1}{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm MACC;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm MACC;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}^{-T}\}.

The components of the asymptotic variance are
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm MACC;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}={\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}
and
{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm MACC;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}=E\{({\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm MACC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})-E\{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm MACC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})/\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}\}[E\{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})/\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}\}]^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0}))^{\otimes 2}\}
. Let
{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm MACC;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}=E\{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm MACC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})/\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}\}
, which becomes

\displaystyle{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm MACC;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}

\displaystyle=E\biggr\{\frac{\partial}{\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm MACC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})\biggr\}

\displaystyle=E\biggr(\Delta\frac{\partial}{\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}}{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})+\frac{\partial}{\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}}\biggr[\biggr\{1-\frac{\delta}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}\biggr\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm MACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\biggr]\biggr)

\displaystyle=E\biggr(\biggr\{1-\frac{\Delta}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}\biggr\}\frac{\partial}{\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm MACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})-\frac{\partial}{\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}}\biggr\{\frac{\delta}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}\biggr\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm MACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\biggr)

\displaystyle=E\biggr[\frac{\Delta}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})^{2}}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm MACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\left\{\frac{\partial}{\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}}\int_{w\leq c}f_{C|{\mathbf{Z}}}(c,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})dc\right\}\biggr].

When we do use
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}
,
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm MACC;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}
becomes
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm MACC;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$},{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}
which still equals
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}
.
The matrix
{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm MACC;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}
becomes
{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm MACC;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$},{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}
. The only difference is that
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm MACC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})
is replaced by
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm MACC;{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})
.
Finally,

\displaystyle E\biggr\{\frac{\partial}{\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm MACC;{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})\biggr\}={\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm MACC;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}.

7.

AIPW estimator

The AIPW estimator,
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\rm AIPW}
, is asymptotically normally distributed. Suppose that
f_{C|{\mathbf{Z}}}
is known as
f_{C|{\mathbf{Z}}}(c,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})
. Then, when
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm AIPW}
is not used,

\displaystyle n^{1/2}(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{{\rm AIPW}}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\rightarrow_{d}\hbox{Normal}\{{\mathbf{0}},{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm AIPW}^{-1}{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm AIPW}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm AIPW}^{-T}\}.

The components of the asymptotic variance are
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm AIPW}=E\{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm AIPW}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})/\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{{}^{\rm T}}\}
and
{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm AIPW}=E\{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm AIPW}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})^{\otimes 2}\}
.
When we do use
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm AIPW}
, then
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm AIPW}
and
{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm AIPW}
become
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm AIPW;{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}
and
{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm AIPW;{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}
, respectively. The only difference is that
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm AIPW}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})
is substituted by
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm AIPW;{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})
.

Note that
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm AIPW}={\mathbf{A}}_{\rm AIPW;{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}={\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}
since

\displaystyle{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm AIPW}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\biggr\{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm AIPW}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})\biggr\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\biggr[\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}}\frac{\Delta{\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}+\biggr\{1-\frac{\delta}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}\biggr\}\biggr\{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\biggr\}\biggr]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\biggr\{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}}\frac{\Delta{\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}\biggr\}\ {\rm since\ the\ second\ part\ equals\ {\mathbf{0}}}

and

\displaystyle{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm AIPW;{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\biggr\{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm AIPW;{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})\biggr\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\biggr[\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}}\frac{\Delta{\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}+\biggr\{1-\frac{\Delta}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}\biggr\}{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm AIPW}\biggr\{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})\biggr\}\biggr]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\biggr\{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}}\frac{\Delta{\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}\biggr\}\ {\rm since\ the\ second\ part\ equals\ {\mathbf{0}}.}

When the nuisance distribution parameters
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}
need to be estimated by estimating
\alpha
as in Section
S.2.1
, then

\displaystyle n^{1/2}(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{{\rm AIPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\rightarrow_{d}\hbox{Normal}\{{\mathbf{0}},{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm AIPW}^{-1}{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm AIPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm AIPW}^{-T}\}.

The components of the asymptotic variance are
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm AIPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}={\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}
and
{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm AIPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}=E\{({\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm AIPW}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})-E\{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm AIPW}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})/\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}\}[E\{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})/\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}\}]^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0}))^{\otimes 2}\}
. Let,
{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm IPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}=E\{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm IPW}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})/\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}\}
. Observe that

\displaystyle E\biggr\{\frac{\partial}{\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm AIPW}({\mathbf{O}},{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})\biggr\}

\displaystyle=E\left\{\frac{\partial}{\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}}\frac{\delta{\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}(y,w,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(w,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}\right\}+E\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}}\biggr[\biggr\{1-\frac{\delta}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(w,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}\biggr\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\biggr]\right)

\displaystyle={\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm IPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}-{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm AIPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}.

When we do use
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm AIPW}
, we now use
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm AIPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$},{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}
and
{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm AIPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$},{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}
. We have that
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm AIPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$},{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}
still equals
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}
. The matrix
{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm AIPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$},{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}
is the same as
{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm AIPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}
, except that

{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm AIPW}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})
changes to
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm AIPW;{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})
.
Finally,

\displaystyle E\biggr\{\frac{\partial}{\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm AIPW;{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})\biggr\}={\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm IPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}-{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm AIPW}{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm AIPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}.

S.2.5

Efficiency

We now show how the augmented estimators were constructed to gain efficiency.

1.

ACC estimator:

When only
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
is unknown and we do not use
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC}
:

We choose
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})
so the ACC estimator is more efficient than the CC estimator. To do so, we compare the influence functions of
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\rm CC}
and
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\rm ACC}
, and we find the form of
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
that makes the efficiency condition in equation (
2
) true. Satisfying the efficiency condition is equivalent to

\displaystyle\hbox{cov}\biggr[\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})+\left\{\Delta-\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})\right\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\biggr]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hbox{cov}\biggr(\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}),\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})+\left\{\Delta-\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})\right\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\biggr).

The left side of the equation is equal to

\displaystyle\hbox{cov}\biggr[\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})+\left\{\Delta-\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})\right\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\biggr]

\displaystyle={\mathbf{B}}_{\rm CC}+{\mathbf{H}}_{\rm ACC}+{\mathbf{H}}_{\rm ACC}^{T}+{\mathbf{D}}_{\rm ACC},

where

\displaystyle{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm CC}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E[\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\{\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\}^{T}],

\displaystyle{\mathbf{H}}_{\rm ACC}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\left(\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\left[\left\{\Delta-\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})\right\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right]^{T}\right),

\displaystyle{\mathbf{D}}_{\rm ACC}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\left(\left[\left\{\Delta-\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})\right\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right]^{\otimes 2}\right).

The right side of the equation is equal to

\displaystyle\hbox{cov}\left[\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}),\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})+\left\{\Delta-\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})\right\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right]

\displaystyle={\mathbf{B}}_{\rm CC}+{\mathbf{H}}_{\rm ACC}.

Therefore,
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
should be chosen such that,
{\mathbf{H}}_{\rm ACC}^{T}+{\mathbf{D}}_{\rm ACC}={\mathbf{0}}
. Equivalently, we may find
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
such that

\displaystyle{\mathbf{0}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{W,\Delta|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left[\left\{\Delta-\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})\right\}\left[\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(y,W,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})+\left\{\Delta-\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})\right\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right]^{T}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{W,\Delta|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left[\left\{\Delta-\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})\right\}\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(y,W,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\quad+E_{W,\Delta|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left[\left\{\Delta-\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})\right\}^{2}\right]{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}.

Solving for
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
leads to

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC,eff}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})

\displaystyle=-E_{W,\Delta|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left[\left\{\Delta-\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})\right\}\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(y,W,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right]E_{W,\Delta|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left[\left\{\Delta-\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})\right\}^{2}\right]^{-T}

\displaystyle=-E_{W,\Delta|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left\{\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(y,W,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right\}\frac{1-\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})}{\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})\{1-\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})\}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{E_{W,\Delta|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left\{\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(y,W,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right\}}{\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})}

\displaystyle\equiv-E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}},\Delta=1}\left\{{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(y,X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right\}.

For this choice of
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
, the ACC estimator is more efficient than the CC estimator. Note that by interchanging the roles of
\Delta
and
R
, we find that the form of
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
is equivalent to that of
Bartlett et al. (2014a)
.

When
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
and
{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}
are unknown and we do not use
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC}
:
When
{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}
is estimated using a logistic regression model with the logit-link function,
Bartlett et al. (2014a)
showed that the same
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
, which is found when
{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}
is assumed known, also ensures higher efficiency over the CC estimator. We refer the reader to
Bartlett et al. (2014a)
for more details. Note that the
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
that guarantees higher efficiency than the CC estimator may be different when estimating
{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}
using a method other than logisitic regression.

When only
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
is unknown and we use
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC}
:
Instead of finding the form of
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
that will make the ACC estimator more efficient than the CC estimator, we find a fixed matrix
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC}
that will make the ACC estimator more efficient than the CC estimator for any choice of
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
. Like before, we compare the influence functions of
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{\rm CC}
and
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}
. We choose a
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC}
that makes the efficiency condition in equation (
2
) true. That efficiency condition is equivalent to

\displaystyle\hbox{cov}\left[\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})+\left\{\Delta-\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})\right\}{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right]

\displaystyle=\hbox{cov}\left[\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}),\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})+\left\{\Delta-\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})\right\}{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right].

Solving for
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC}
leads to

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC}

\displaystyle=-E\left[\left\{\Delta-\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})\right\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times E\left(\left[\left\{\Delta-\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})\right\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right]^{\otimes 2}\right)^{-T}.

For any choice of
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
, the above
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC}
guarantees higher efficiency over the CC estimator. The sample mean
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC}
is used in the estimation of
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
. This is done by first obtaining an estimate of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
using a consistent estimator (e.g., the CC estimator), then using this estimate to compute

\displaystyle\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}_{\rm ACC}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\biggr(\left[\left\{\delta_{i}-\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y_{i},{\mathbf{z}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})\right\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(y_{i},{\mathbf{z}}_{i};\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}})\right]\delta_{i}{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(y_{i},w_{i},{\mathbf{z}}_{i};\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}})^{T}\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\times\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\left\{\delta_{i}-\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y_{i},{\mathbf{z}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})\right\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(y_{i},{\mathbf{z}}_{i};\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}})\right]^{\otimes 2}\right)^{-T}.

Since
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC}
only affects the variance of
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
and not its consistency, then by Theorem 6.2 of
Newey & McFadden (1994a)
, the influence function
{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}
may be used in the estimation of
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
under the presence of
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}_{\rm ACC}
. This result means that the uncertainty of
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}_{\rm ACC}
may be ignored in the variance estimation of
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}
.

When
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
and
{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}
are unknown and we use
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC}
:
We use a similar approach as that used when only
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
is unknown. This time, we choose a
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC}
that makes the efficiency condition in equation (
2
) true for any choice of
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})
.
We will compare the influence functions of
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{\rm CC}
and
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}},{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}}
, and find
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}}
that makes the efficiency condition in equation (
2
) true. Let
{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}})=-E\{\partial{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})/\partial{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}^{T}\}^{-1}{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})
be the influence function of
{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}
, and
{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}}=E\{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})/\partial{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}^{T}\}
as derived when establishing the asymptotic normality of the ACC estimator. Then, the efficiency condition is equivalent to

\displaystyle\hbox{cov}\left(\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})+{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}}\left[\left\{\Delta-\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})\right\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})+{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}}{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}})\right]\right)

\displaystyle=\hbox{cov}(\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}),

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})+{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}}\left[\left\{\Delta-\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})\right\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})+{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}}{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}})\right]).

Solving for
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}}
leads to

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}}

\displaystyle=-E\biggr(\left[\left\{\Delta-\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})\right\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})+{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}}{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}})\right]\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})^{T}\biggr)

\displaystyle\times E\left(\left[\left\{\Delta-\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})\right\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})+{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}}{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}})\right]^{\otimes 2}\right)^{-T}.

For any choice of
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})
, the above
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}}
guarantees higher efficiency over the CC estimator. The sample mean
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}}
