is used in the estimation of
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
. That sample mean is

\displaystyle\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\biggr(\left[\left\{\delta_{i}-\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y_{i},{\mathbf{z}}_{i};\widehat{\boldsymbol{\kappa}})\right\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(y_{i},{\mathbf{z}}_{i};\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}})+{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}}{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}}(y_{i},{\mathbf{z}}_{i})\right]\delta_{i}{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(y_{i},w_{i},{\mathbf{z}}_{i};\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}})^{T}\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\times\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\left\{\delta_{i}-\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y_{i},{\mathbf{z}}_{i};\widehat{\boldsymbol{\kappa}})\right\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(y_{i},{\mathbf{z}}_{i};\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}})+{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}}{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}}(y_{i},{\mathbf{z}}_{i})\right]^{\otimes 2}\right)^{-T},

where
(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\kappa}})
are obtained from consistent estimators (e.g., CC estimator and logistic regression). Since
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}}
only affects the variance of
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
and not its consistency, the influence function
{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}},{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}}
may be used in the estimation of
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
when we include
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}}
(
Newey & McFadden, 1994a
, Theorem 6.2)
.
The uncertainty of
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}}
may thus be ignored in the variance estimation of
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}},{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}}
.

2.

MACC estimator:

When only
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
is unknown and we do not use
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}
:

We follow a similar process as that used with the ACC estimator, except this time, we compare the influence functions of
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\rm CC}
and
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\rm MACC}
, and we find the form of
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm MACC}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
that makes the efficiency condition in equation (
2
) true. Satisfying the efficiency condition requires

\displaystyle{\mathbf{0}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{W,\Delta|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\biggr(\left\{1-\frac{\Delta}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\times\biggr[\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(y,W,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})+\left\{1-\frac{\Delta}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}\right\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm MACC}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\biggr]^{T}\biggr)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{W,\Delta|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left[\left\{1-\frac{\Delta}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}\right\}\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(y,W,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+E_{W,\Delta|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left[\left\{1-\frac{\Delta}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}\right\}^{2}\right]{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm MACC}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}.

Solving for
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm MACC}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
results in:

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm MACC,eff}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})

\displaystyle\quad=-E_{W,\Delta|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left[\left\{1-\frac{\Delta}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}\right\}\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(y,W,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right]E_{W,\Delta|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left[\left\{1-\frac{\Delta}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}\right\}^{2}\right]^{-T}

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left[\left\{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})-1\right\}{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(y,X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right]}{E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left\{1-1/\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})\right\}}.

With this choice of
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm MACC}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
, the MACC estimator is more efficient than the CC estimator.

When
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
and
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}
are unknown and we do not use
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}
:
Unlike the ACC estimator, the MACC estimator may require a different formulation of
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm MACC}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
to guarantee efficiency gains over the CC estimator. This difference means that the current choice of
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
may not guarantee the efficiency condition in equation (
2
). Finding a
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm MACC}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
that guarantees efficiency over the CC estimator when
\alpha
is estimated using the strategy discussed in Section
S.2.1
is challenging. The difficulty is that we cannot easily solve for
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm MACC}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
because
{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm MACC;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}
is an expectation of a function on
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm MACC}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
. Therefore, we recommend using the MACC estimator with
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}
over the regular MACC estimator, as using
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}
will guarantee higher efficiency than the CC estimator regardless of the choice of
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm MACC}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
.

When only
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
is unknown and we use
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}
:

We compare the influence functions of
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{\rm CC}
and
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{\rm MACC;{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}
to find a fixed matrix
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}
that will make the MACC estimator more efficient than the CC estimator for any choice of
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm MACC}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
. To achieve that goal, we strive to find
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}
that makes the following efficiency condition in equation (
2
) true.
Equivalently,

\displaystyle\hbox{cov}\left[\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})+\biggr\{1-\frac{\Delta}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}\biggr\}{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm MACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right]

\displaystyle=\hbox{cov}\biggr(\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}),\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})+\biggr\{1-\frac{\Delta}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}\biggr\}{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm MACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\biggr]\biggr).

Solving for
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}
leads to

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}

\displaystyle=-E\biggr[\biggr\{1-\frac{\Delta}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}\biggr\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm MACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}\biggr]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times E\biggr(\biggr[\biggr\{1-\frac{\Delta}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm MACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\biggr]^{\otimes 2}\biggr)^{-T}.

This choice of
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}
ensures that the MACC estimator is more efficient than the CC estimator for any choice of
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm MACC}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
. In practice, we estimate
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}
by its sample mean,
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}
, which is

\displaystyle\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}_{\rm MACC}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\biggr(\left[\biggr\{1-\frac{\delta}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(w,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}\biggr\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm MACC}(y_{i},{\mathbf{z}}_{i};\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}})\right]\delta_{i}{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(y_{i},w_{i},{\mathbf{z}}_{i};\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}})^{T}\biggr)

\displaystyle\quad\times\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\biggr\{1-\frac{\delta}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(w,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}\biggr\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm MACC}(y_{i},{\mathbf{z}}_{i};\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}})\right]^{\otimes 2}\right)^{-T},

where the estimates
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
are from a consistent estimator (e.g., the CC estimator).
As we saw with the ACC estimator,
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}
affects the variance, but not the consistency of
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
. When using
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}_{\rm MACC}
, we may thus use the influence function
{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\rm MACC;{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}
when estimating
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
. Consequently, the uncertainty of
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}_{\rm MACC}
may be ignored in the variance estimation of
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{\rm MACC;{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}
.

When
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
and
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}
are unknown and we use
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}
:

Similar to when only
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
is unknown, we strive to find a fixed matrix
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}
that makes the MACC estimator more efficient than the CC estimator for any choice of
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm MACC}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
. Let
{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*})=-E\{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$})/\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}\}^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$})
. Then, to satisfy the efficiency condition, we find that using

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}

\displaystyle=-E\biggr(\biggr[\biggr\{1-\frac{\Delta}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}\biggr\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm MACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})+{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm MACC;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*})\biggr]\delta{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}\biggr)^{T}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times E\biggr(\biggr[\biggr\{1-\frac{\Delta}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}\biggr\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm MACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})+{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm MACC;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*})\biggr]^{\otimes 2}\biggr)^{-T}

will guarantee the MACC estimator is more efficient than the CC estimator for any choice of
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm MACC}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
. When estimating
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
, we use the sample mean estimate of
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}
, denoted by
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}
. Like we did when computing
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}
, we obtain
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}
by replacing
({\boldsymbol{\theta}},{\boldsymbol{\eta}})
with estimates
(\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\widehat{\boldsymbol{\eta}})
obtained from a consistent estimator (e.g., the CC estimator and the estimator from Section
S.2.1
) and replacing expectations with summands.
Similar to our argument when only
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
is unknown, the uncertainty of
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}_{\rm MACC;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}
may be ignored in the variance estimation of
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{\rm MACC;{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}
.

3.

AIPW estimator:

When only
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
is unknown and we do not use
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm AIPW}
:
By comparing the influence functions of
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{\rm IPW}
and
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{\rm AIPW}
, we find the form of
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
that makes the AIPW estimator more efficient than the IPW estimator. This gain in efficiency is satisfied when
{\mathbf{0}}=\hbox{cov}({\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\rm AIPW}-{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\rm IPW},{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\rm AIPW})
. Applying a similar argument as that for the MACC estimator, we find that using

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW,eff}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})

\displaystyle\quad=-E_{W,\Delta|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left[\left\{1-\frac{\Delta}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}\right\}\frac{\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(y,W,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}\right]E_{W,\Delta|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left[\left\{1-\frac{\Delta}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}\right\}^{2}\right]^{-T}

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left[\left\{1-1/\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})\right\}{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(y,X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right]}{E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left\{1-1/\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})\right\}}

will ensure the AIPW estimator is more efficient than the IPW estimator.

When
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
and
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}
are unknown and we do not use
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm AIPW}
:
Similar to the MACC estimator for unknown
f_{C|{\mathbf{Z}}}
, the same
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
does not guarantee the efficiency condition in equation (
2
). Finding the form of
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})
that satisfies the efficiency condition is non-trivial since
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})
is embedded in an expectation, i.e.,
{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm AIPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}
. Therefore, we recommend using the AIPW estimator with
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm AIPW}
, as that estimator will have higher efficiency than the IPW estimator for any choice of
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
.

When only
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
is unknown and we use
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm AIPW}
:

Instead of finding the form of
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
that will make the AIPW estimator more efficient than the IPW estimator, we find a fixed matrix
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm AIPW}
that makes the AIPW estimator more efficient than the IPW estimator for any choice of
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
. As before, we compare the influence functions of
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{\rm IPW}
and
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{\rm AIPW}
and find the form of
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm AIPW}
that satisfies the efficiency condition in equation
2
. Following similar calculations to those in ACC and MACC, we find this efficiency condition in equation
2
is satisfied when

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm AIPW}

\displaystyle=-E\biggr[\biggr\{1-\frac{\Delta}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}\biggr\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\frac{\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}\biggr]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times E\biggr(\biggr[\biggr\{1-\frac{\Delta}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}\biggr\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\biggr]^{\otimes 2}\biggr)^{-T}.

We use the sample mean of
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm AIPW}
, denoted by
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm AIPW}
, when estimating
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
. That sample mean is

\displaystyle\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}_{\rm AIPW}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\biggr\{1-\frac{\delta_{i}}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(w_{i},{\mathbf{z}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}\biggr\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW}(y_{i},{\mathbf{z}}_{i};\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}})\frac{\delta_{i}{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(y_{i},w_{i},{\mathbf{z}}_{i};\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}})^{T}}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(w_{i},{\mathbf{z}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\times\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\biggr\{1-\frac{\delta_{i}}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(w_{i},{\mathbf{z}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}\biggr\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW}(y_{i},{\mathbf{z}}_{i};\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}})\right]^{\otimes 2}\right)^{-T},

where
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
is from a consistent estimator (e.g., the IPW estimator). Following similar arguments to those used for the ACC and MACC estimators, the uncertainty of
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}_{\rm AIPW}
may be ignored in the variance estimation of
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{\rm AIPW;{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}
.

When
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
and
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}
are unknown and we use
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm AIPW}
:
We follow a similar argument as that when only
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
is unknown, except that now we find the form of
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm AIPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}
that makes the efficiency condition in equation (
2
) true. Equivalently,

\displaystyle{\mathbf{0}}

\displaystyle=\hbox{cov}\biggr[{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm AIPW;{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})+({\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm IPW,\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}+{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm AIPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm AIPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}){\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad-\biggr\{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm IPW}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})+{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm IPW,\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*})\biggr\},

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm AIPW;{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})+({\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm IPW,\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}+{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm AIPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm AIPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}){\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*})\biggr]

\displaystyle=\hbox{cov}\biggr({\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm AIPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}\biggr[\biggr\{1-\frac{\Delta}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})}\biggr\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})+{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm AIPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*})\biggr],

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm AIPW;{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})+({\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW,\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}+{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm AIPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm AIPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}){\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*})\biggr).

Solving for
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm AIPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}
leads to

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm AIPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}

\displaystyle=-\biggr\{E\biggr(\left[\biggr\{1-\frac{\Delta}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})}\biggr\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})+{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm AIPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*})\right]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\times\left\{\frac{\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})}+{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm IPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*})\right\}\biggr)\biggr\}^{T}

\displaystyle\times E\biggr(\biggr[\biggr\{1-\frac{\Delta}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})}\biggr\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})+{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm AIPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*})\biggr]^{\otimes 2}\biggr)^{-T}.

For any choice of
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
, the above
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm AIPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}
guarantees higher efficiency over the IPW estimator. The sample mean of
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm AIPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}
is used in the estimation of
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
, which is computed by replacing expectations with summands in
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm AIPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}
and replacing
({\boldsymbol{\theta}},{\boldsymbol{\eta}})
with estimates obtained from a consistent estimator for each (e.g.,the CC estimator and the estimator described in Section
S.2.1
).
Following arguments similar to those for the ACC and MACC estimators, the uncertainty of
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}}_{\rm AIPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}
may be ignored in the variance estimation of
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{\rm AIPW;{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}
.

S.2.6

Changes to accommodate dependent right-censoring

Up to now, we assumed independent covariate right-censoring. Now we show the changes to the estimators when we assume dependent covariate right-censoring.

1.

Nuisance distribution estimators:

As explained in Section
5
,
\alpha
is non-identifiable without additional assumptions beyond those made for independent covariate right-censoring. For
\alpha
to be identifiable under dependent covariate right-censoring, we require that the true conditional covariance between
X
and
C
given
{\mathbf{Z}}
is known and that
f_{C,X|{\mathbf{Z}}}
is sufficiently smooth around
\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0}
. Under this assumption, a parametric distribution can be imposed for the log-likelihood and the decomposition can be reduced to

\displaystyle\hbox{log}f_{W,\Delta|{\mathbf{Z}}}(w_{i},\delta_{i},{\mathbf{z}}_{i};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$})

\displaystyle=\ \delta_{i}\hbox{log}\biggr\{\int_{w_{i}\leq C}f_{C|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(c,w_{i},{\mathbf{z}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{*})f_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}(w_{i},{\mathbf{z}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{*})dc\biggr\}+

\displaystyle\quad\quad(1-\delta_{i})\hbox{log}\biggr\{\int_{w_{i}\leq X}f_{C|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(w_{i},x,{\mathbf{z}}_{i}{\boldsymbol{\eta}}^{*})f_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}(x,{\mathbf{z}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{*})dx\biggr\}.

The main difference in this decomposition compared to that when covariate right-censoring is
independent
is in the conditional decomposition of
f_{C,X|{\mathbf{Z}}}(c,w_{i},{\mathbf{z}}_{i};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$})
. Since the covariance between
X
and
C
given
{\mathbf{Z}}
is assumed to be known (not zero), the vector
\alpha
that needs to be estimated remains the same. Therefore, the proof of consistency and asymptotic normality remains the same under dependent covariate right-censoring.

2.

CC estimator:

The estimating equation for the CC estimator is only a function of
f_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}
, therefore, the CC estimator is independent of
f_{C,X|{\mathbf{Z}}}
. As a result, the CC estimator does not change when covariate right-censoring is dependent. Therefore, the proof for consistency and asymptotic normality, as well as the proof for robustness remain the same

3.

IPW estimator:

Under dependent covariate right-censoring, the IPW estimating equations use the probabilities defined by
\pi^{\rm cens,dep}_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(w,{\mathbf{z}})=\int_{w\leq C}f_{C|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(w,{\mathbf{z}})dc
as opposed to the probabilities defined by
\pi^{\rm cens,ind}_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(w,{\mathbf{z}})=\int_{w\leq C}f_{C|{\mathbf{Z}}}(w,{\mathbf{z}})dc
under independent covariate right-censoring. Both probabilities remain a function of
(X,{\mathbf{Z}})
, therefore, by interchanging the roles of
\pi^{\rm cens,dep}_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(w,{\mathbf{z}})
and
\pi^{\rm cens,ind}_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(w,{\mathbf{z}})
, the proof for consistency and asymptotic normality, as well as the proof for robustness remain the same. The consistency and asymptotic normality of
\alpha
requires knowing the true conditional covariance of
(X,C)
given
{\mathbf{Z}}
.

4.

MLE:

Under dependent covariate right-censoring, the MLE estimating equations use the density
f_{X|C,{\mathbf{Z}}}
as opposed to
f_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}
. By interchanging the roles of
f_{X|C,{\mathbf{Z}}}
and
f_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}
, the proof for consistency and asymptotic normality, as well as the proof for robustness remain the same. The consistency and asymptotic normality of
\alpha
requires knowing the true conditional covariance of
(X,C)
given
{\mathbf{Z}}
.

5.

ACC estimator:

The ACC estimator uses the probability
\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}
which may be specified/estimated without involving
f_{X,C|{\mathbf{Z}}}
. Therefore, the proof for consistency and asymptotic normality, as well as the proof for robustness remain the same under dependent covariate right-censoring. These results hold true even when we use
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC}
as this is a non-random matrix.

6.

MACC and AIPW estimators:

Under dependent covariate right-censoring, the estimating equation for the MACC and AIPW estimators use the density
f_{C|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}
as opposed to
f_{C|{\mathbf{Z}}}
to define the probability
\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(w,{\mathbf{z}};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$})
. By interchanging the roles of
f_{C|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}
and
f_{C|{\mathbf{Z}}}
, the proof for consistency and asymptotic normality, as well as the proof for robustness remain the same. The consistency and asymptotic normality of
\alpha
requires knowing the true conditional covariance of
(X,C)
given
{\mathbf{Z}}
. These results hold true even when we use
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}
and
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm AIPW}
, as these are non-random matrices.

S.3

Proof of Theorem
1
for the missing covariate problem

S.3.1

Specification or estimation of nuisance distributions

Similar to the right-censored covariate problem, estimation of
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
under the presence of a missing covariate requires the specification or estimation of nuisance parameters as discussed in Section
5
. For example, under independent covariate missingness:

•

IPW estimator: Requires the density
f_{R|{\mathbf{Z}}}
to compute the probability
\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{z}})
;

•

MLE: Requires the density
f_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}(x,{\mathbf{z}})
;

•

ACC, MACC, and AIPW estimators: Requires the density
f_{R|{\mathbf{Z}}}
to compute the probability
\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{z}})
and
f_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}
to compute
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})
.

Throughout, we consider the parametric models
f_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}(x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\iota}})
and
f_{R|{\mathbf{Z}}}(r,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}})
, where
({\boldsymbol{\iota}}^{T},{\boldsymbol{\tau}}^{T})^{T}
is a set of finite-dimensional parameters. Under a
mechanistic
approach, the parameter space is assumed known, but in practice, these parameters need to be estimated. We point out when a consistent estimator of these nuisance distribution is required. In what follows, we focus on proving the consistency and asymptotic properties of the estimators, as well as defining their robustness properties.

S.3.2

Consistency

1.

CC estimator:

Observe that

\displaystyle E\left\{R{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}[E_{XR,R|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left\{R{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right\}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}[E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left\{\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0}){\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right\}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}[\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})E_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\}]={\mathbf{0}}.

Therefore, the CC estimating equation is unbiased, and the resulting estimator is consistent.

2.

IPW estimator:

Observe that

\displaystyle E\left\{\frac{R{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})}{\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\biggr[E_{XR,R|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left\{\frac{R{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})}{\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})}\right\}\biggr]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\biggr[E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left\{\frac{\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})}{\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})}\times{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right\}+{\mathbf{0}}\biggr]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left\{{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right\}={\mathbf{0}}.

Therefore, the IPW estimating equation is unbiased, and the resulting estimator is consistent.

3.

MLE:

The nuisance distribution for MLE in both the right-censored and missing covariate problems is
f_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}
. Therefore, the proof of consistency remains the same as that shown for the right-censored covariate problem. The only difference is that the domain of integration covers the entire domain of
X
in the missing covariate problem.

4.

ACC estimator:

We previously proved the CC estimating equation is unbiased. It thus suffices to show that the expectation of the augmentation term is equal to
{\mathbf{0}}
. It follows that

\displaystyle E_{Y,XR,R,{\mathbf{Z}}}[\{R-\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})]

\displaystyle=E_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}[E_{XR,R|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{R-\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})].

Consistency is achieved if
E_{XR,R|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{R-\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})\}={\mathbf{0}}
. Using conditional expectation rules it follows that

\displaystyle E_{XR,R|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{R-\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{XR,R|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(R)-\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}})-\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})-\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})=0.

The last line follows since
\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}})
is reduced to
\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})
. Since
E_{XR,R|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{R-\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})\}=0
then it follows that
E_{Y,XR,R,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm ACC}({\mathbf{O}};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})\}={\mathbf{0}}
. Since
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC}
is a non-random matrix, the proof of robustness stays the same when modifying the ACC to include
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC}
.

5.

MACC estimator:

As noted in the main manuscript, the estimating equations of the ACC and MACC estimators are identical for the choice of
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm MACC,eff}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})
. Therefore, the proof of consistency is the same as that for the ACC estimator. Since
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}
is a non-random matrix, the proof of consistency stays the same when modifying the MACC estimator to include
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}
.

6.

AIPW estimator:

The AIPW estimating equations use the IPW estimating equation and an augmentation part. We previously showed that the IPW estimating equations have mean zero. Therefore, it remains to be shown that the augmentation part of the AIPW estimator has mean
{\mathbf{0}}
to prove consistency. The augmentation term
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})
is any function of
(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})
, therefore by interchanging the roles of
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})
and
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm MACC}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})
, the proof of consistency remains the same as for the ACC and MACC estimators. Since
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm AIPW}
is a non-random matrix, the proof of robustness stays the same when modifying the AIPW to include
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm AIPW}
.

S.3.3

Robustness

1.

CC estimator:

As noted in previous works, such as
Little (1992a)
and
Ashner & Garcia (2024a)
, the CC estimator is valid as long as the missingness is independent (or conditionally independent) of the outcome. In our paper we assume that
Y\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}(C,R)|X,{\mathbf{Z}}
, meaning that the right-censoring and missingness are conditionally independent of the outcome given
(X,{\mathbf{Z}})
. Therefore, the same robustness and asymptotic properties apply when interchanging the roles of
\Delta
and
R
. That is, the CC estimator is robust to misspecification of
f_{R|{\mathbf{Z}}}
,
f_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}
, and
f_{{\mathbf{Z}}}
.

2.

IPW estimator:

Consider the case that
f_{R|{\mathbf{Z}}}
is incorrectly specified as
f_{R|{\mathbf{Z}}}^{*}
. Let
\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}^{*}({\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})=f_{R|{\mathbf{Z}}}^{*}(r=1,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})
. Then,

\displaystyle E\left\{\frac{R{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})}{\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\biggr[E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left\{\frac{\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}^{*}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})}{\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})}\times{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right\}\biggr]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}\biggr[\frac{\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}^{*}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})}{\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})}\times E_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left\{{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right\}\biggr]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}\biggr[\frac{\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}^{*}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})}{\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})}\times{\mathbf{0}}\biggr]={\mathbf{0}}.

The last line follows since
E_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left\{{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})\right\}={\mathbf{0}}
. Therefore, the IPW estimator is robust to the misspecification of
f_{R|{\mathbf{Z}}}
,
f_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}
, and
f_{{\mathbf{Z}}}
.

3.

MLE:

The only nuisance distribution in the MLE is
f_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}
. Therefore, the proof of robustness remains the same when changing the domain of integration for the missing covariate setting to the entire domain of
X
as opposed that used for the right-censored covariate problem, i.e.,
X<C
. We conclude that the the MLE is robust to the misspecficiation of
f_{R|{\mathbf{Z}}}
and
f_{{\mathbf{Z}}}
, but not
f_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}
.

4.

ACC estimator:

To show that the ACC is doubly robust under independent missingness, it suffices to show that the estimator is consistent when the probabilities are correctly specified or the augmentation component is correctly specified. We let
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})=-E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(y,X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\}
. Later, we show that this choice makes the ACC estimator more efficient than the CC estimator. Consider the case that
\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})
is incorrectly specified as
\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}^{*}({\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})
but
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm ACC}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
is correctly specified:

\displaystyle E_{Y,XR,R,{\mathbf{Z}}}[R{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})-\{R-\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}^{*}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})\}E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\}]

\displaystyle=-E_{Y,XR,R,{\mathbf{Z}}}[\{R-\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}^{*}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})\}E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\}]

\displaystyle=-E_{Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}}}[\{\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})-\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}^{*}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})\}E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\}]

\displaystyle=-E_{Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}}}[\{\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})-\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}^{*}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})\}{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})]

\displaystyle=-E_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}[\{\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})-\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}^{*}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})\}E_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\}]

\displaystyle=-E_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}[\{\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})-\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}^{*}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})\}\times{\mathbf{0}}\}]={\mathbf{0}}.

The first line follows since the CC estimating equations are unbiased. The second line follows by the law of conditional expectation.
The last two lines follow from the law of conditional expectation and since
E_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\}={\mathbf{0}}
. Now consider the case when the probability
\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}})
is correctly specified but
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm ACC}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
is incorrectly specified as
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm ACC}^{*}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
. It follows that

\displaystyle E_{Y,XR,R,{\mathbf{Z}}}[R{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})+\{R-\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})\}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm ACC}^{*}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\}]

\displaystyle=E_{Y,XR,R,{\mathbf{Z}}}[\{R-\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})\}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm ACC}^{*}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\}]

\displaystyle=E_{Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}}}[\{\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})-\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})\}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm ACC}^{*}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})]

\displaystyle=E_{Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{0\times{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm ACC}^{*}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\}={\mathbf{0}}.

Therefore we have proven that the ACC estimator only requires correct specification of either the probabilities or the augmentation component. Consequently, the ACC estimator is doubly robust under independent covariate missingness. Since
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC}
is a non-random matrix, the same robustness properties apply as those of the ACC estimator without
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC}
.

5.

MACC estimator:

The estimating equations of the ACC and MACC estimators are identical for the choice of
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm MACC}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})
. Therefore, the same robustness properties apply for the independent missingness case. Since
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}
is a non-random matrix, the robustness properties with and without using
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}
are the same.

6.

AIPW estimator:

To show that the AIPW estimator is doubly robust under independent missingness, it suffices to show that the estimator is consistent when the probabilities are correctly specified or the augmentation component is correctly specified. We let
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})=E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(y,x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\}
. We will later show that this choice makes the AIPW estimator more efficient than the IPW estimator. Since
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})=-{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
, then we can show that the AIPW estimator is doubly robust using a similar argument as that which we used for the ACC estimator. The robustness properties do not change when using
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm AIPW}
since
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm AIPW}
is a non-random matrix.

S.3.4

Asymptotic normality

1.

CC estimator:

By interchanging the roles of
(W,\Delta)
with
(XR,R)
, the proof of asymptotic normality remains the same.

2.

IPW estimator:

By interchanging
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm IPW}({\mathbf{O}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})
from the missing covariate problem with
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm IPW}({\mathbf{O}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})
from the right-censored covariate problem, the proof of asymptotic normality remains the same when
f_{R|{\mathbf{Z}}}
is known. When
f_{R|{\mathbf{Z}}}
needs to be estimated, the corresponding consistent and asymptotically normal estimator
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\tau}}
needs to be specified. Then, the influence function of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\tau}}
is used in the construction of the asymptotic normality of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\rm IPW;{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}
.

3.

MLE:

By interchanging
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm MLE}({\mathbf{O}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\iota}}_{0})
from the missing covariate setting with
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm MLE}({\mathbf{O}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{0})
from the right-censored covariate problem, the proof of asymptotic normality remains the same when
f_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}
is known. When,
f_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}
needs to be estimated, the corresponding consistent and asymptotically normal estimator
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\iota}}
needs to be specified. Then, the influence function of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\iota}}
is used in the construction of the asymptotic normality of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\rm MLE;{\boldsymbol{\iota}}}
.

4.

ACC estimator:

By interchanging
\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})
from the missing covariate with
\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\kappa}}_{0})
from the right-censored covariate problem, as well as the augmented components
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})
for both problems, the proof of asymptotic normality remains the same when
f_{R|{\mathbf{Z}}}
is known. When
f_{R|{\mathbf{Z}}}
needs to be estimated, the corresponding consistent and asymptotically normal estimator
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\tau}}
needs to be specified. Then, the influence function of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\tau}}
is used in the construction of the asymptotic normality of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}
when using and when not using
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC}
.

5.

MACC estimator:

As noted in the main manuscript, the estimating equations of the ACC and MACC estimator are identical for the choice of
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm MACC,eff}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})
. Therefore, the same asymptotic properties apply for the independent missingness case, and likewise, when we use
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}
.

6.

AIPW estimator:

By interchanging
\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})
from the missing covariate problem with
\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(w,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})
from the right-censored covariate problem, as well as the augmented components
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})
for both problems, the proof of asymptotic normality remains the same when
f_{R|{\mathbf{Z}}}
is known. When
f_{R|{\mathbf{Z}}}
needs to be estimated, the corresponding consistent and asymptotically normal estimator
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\tau}}
needs to be specified. Then, the influence function of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\tau}}
is used in the construction of the asymptotic normality of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\rm AIPW;{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}
when using and when not using
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm AIPW}
.

S.3.5

Efficiency

For brevity, in this section we show what the forms of the augmented components are for the missing covariate problem after applying a similar argument as that for the right-censored covariate problem. After finding the form of
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}
that guarantees efficiency over the original estimators, one can use the sample mean in the estimation of
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
.

1.

ACC estimator:

When
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
is the only unknown parameter and we do not use
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC}
:
After applying a similar argument as that for the right-censored covariate case, we find

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC,eff}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})

\displaystyle\quad=-E_{XR,R|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left[\left\{R-\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})\right\}R{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(y,X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right]E_{XR,R|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left[\left\{R-\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})\right\}^{2}\right]^{-T}

\displaystyle\quad=-\frac{\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})\{1-\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})\}E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left\{{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(y,X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right\}}{\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})\{1-\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})\}}

\displaystyle\quad=-E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left\{{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(y,X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right\}.

When
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
and
{\boldsymbol{\tau}}
are unknown parameters and we do not use
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC}
:
Using a similar argument as that of
Bartlett et al. (2014a)
, it follows that the same
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm ACC}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})
can be used to guarantee efficiency gains over the CC estimator when
{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0}
is estimated using logistic regression.

When only
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
is unknown and we use
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC}
:
After applying a similar argument as that for the right-censored covariate case, we find

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC}

\displaystyle=-E\left[\left\{R-\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})\right\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})R{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\left(E\left[\left\{R-\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})\right\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right]^{\otimes 2}\right)^{-T}.

When
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
and
{\boldsymbol{\tau}}
are unknown and we use
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC}
:
Before introducing the form of
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}
that guarantees higher efficiency over the CC estimator, we let

\displaystyle{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\left\{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm ACC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})/\partial{\boldsymbol{\tau}}^{T}\right\};

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}({\mathbf{z}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-E\left\{\partial{\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})/\partial{\boldsymbol{\tau}}^{T}\right\}^{-1}{\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}({\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0}),

where
{\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}({\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}})
is the score equation of the log-likelihood of
f_{R|{\mathbf{Z}}}
. Then it follows that,

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}

\displaystyle=-E\biggr(\left[\left\{R-\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})\right\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})+{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}({\mathbf{Z}})\right]R{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})^{T}\biggr)

\displaystyle\times\left(E\left[\left\{R-\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})\right\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm ACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})+{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}({\mathbf{Z}})\right]^{\otimes 2}\right)^{-T}.

2.

MACC estimator:

When
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
is the only unknown parameter and we do not use
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}
:
After applying a similar argument as that for the right-censored covariate case, we find

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm MACC,eff}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})

\displaystyle\quad=-E_{XR,R|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left[\left\{1-R/\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})\right\}R{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(y,X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right]E_{XR,R|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left[\left\{1-R/\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})\right\}^{2}\right]^{-T}

\displaystyle\quad=-\frac{\{\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})-1\}E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left\{{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(y,X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right\}}{-1+1/\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})}

\displaystyle\quad=-\frac{\{\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})-1\}E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left\{{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(y,X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right\}}{-1+1/\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})}

\displaystyle\quad=E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left\{{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(y,X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right\}\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0}).

When
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
and
{\boldsymbol{\tau}}
are unknown parameters and we do not use
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}
:
Using a similar argument as that for the ACC estimator, and assuming that
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}
and
{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0}
are independent, it follows that the same
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm MACC}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})
can be used to guarantee efficiency gains over the CC estimator.

When only
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
is unknown and we use
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}
:

After applying a similar argument as that for the ACC estimator, we find

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}

\displaystyle=-E\left[\left\{1-R/\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})\right\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm MACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})R{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}\right]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\left(E\left[\left\{1-R/\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})\right\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm MACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right]^{\otimes 2}\right)^{-1}.

When
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
and
{\boldsymbol{\tau}}
are unknown and we use
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}
:

Before introducing the form of
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm ACC;{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}
that guarantees higher efficiency over the CC estimator, we let

\displaystyle{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm MACC;{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\left\{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm MACC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})/\partial{\boldsymbol{\tau}}^{T}\right\}.

Then it follows that

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC;{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}

\displaystyle=-E\biggr(\left[\left\{1-R/\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})\right\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm MACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})+{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm MACC;{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}({\mathbf{Z}})\right]R{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}\biggr)

\displaystyle\times\left(E\left[\left\{1-R/\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})\right\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm MACC}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})+{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm MACC;{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}({\mathbf{Z}})\right]^{\otimes 2}\right)^{-T}.

3.

AIPW estimator:

When only
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
is unknown and we do not use
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}
:
We investigate a choice of
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
to make the AIPW estimator more efficient than the IPW estimator. To do so, we compare the influence functions of
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{\rm AIPW}
and
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{\rm IPW}
, and find a choice of
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
such that
{\mathbf{0}}=\hbox{cov}({\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\rm AIPW}-{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\rm IPW},{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\rm AIPW})
. Applying a similar argument as before, we find

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW,eff}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})

\displaystyle\quad=-E_{XR,R|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left[\left\{1-R/\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})\right\}R{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(y,X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})/\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})\right]E_{XR,R|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left[\left\{1-r/\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})\right\}^{2}\right]^{-T}

\displaystyle\quad=-\frac{\{1-1/\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})\}E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left\{{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(y,X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right\}}{\{1/\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}(w,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})-1\}}

\displaystyle\quad=E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left\{{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(y,X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right\}.

When
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
and
{\boldsymbol{\tau}}
are unknown parameters and we do not use
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm AIPW}
:
Using a similar argument as that for the ACC estimator, and assuming that
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}
and
{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0}
are independent, it follows that the same
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm AIPW}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})
can be used to guarantee efficiency gains over the IPW estimator.

When only
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
is unknown and we use
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm AIPW}
:
By interchanging the roles of
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm MACC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})
with
{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm AIPW}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})
, the same argument applies. We find that

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm AIPW}

\displaystyle=-E\left[\left\{1-R/\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})\right\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})R{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}/\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})\right]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\left(E\left[\left\{1-R/\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})\right\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right]^{\otimes 2}\right)^{-T}.

When
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
and
{\boldsymbol{\tau}}
are unknown and we use
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}
:
Before introducing the form of
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm AIPW;{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}
that guarantees higher efficiency over the IPW estimator, we let

\displaystyle{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm IPW;{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\left\{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm IPW}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})/\partial{\boldsymbol{\tau}}^{T}\right\};

\displaystyle{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm AIPW;{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\left[\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\tau}}^{T}}\left\{1-\frac{R}{\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}})}\right\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\right].

We find that

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm AIPW;{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-E\biggr(\left[\left\{1-R/\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})\right\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})+{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm AIPW;{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}({\mathbf{Z}})\right]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\times\{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm IPW}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})+{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm IPW;{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}({\mathbf{Z}})\}\biggr)^{T}

\displaystyle\times E\left(\left[\left\{1-R/\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\tau}}_{0})\right\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})+{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm AIPW;{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{{\boldsymbol{\tau}}}({\mathbf{Z}})\right]^{\otimes 2}\right)^{-1}.

S.3.6

Changes to accommodate dependent covariate missingness

1.

CC estimator:

No changes occur.

2.

IPW estimator:

Under dependent covariate missingness, the IPW estimating equations use the probabilities defined by
\pi^{\rm miss,dep}_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(x,{\mathbf{z}})
as opposed to
\pi^{\rm miss,ind}_{{\mathbf{Z}}}({\mathbf{z}})
. The probabilities in the dependent covariate right-censoring and missingness settings are now a function of
(X,{\mathbf{Z}})
. Therefore, the proofs for consistency and robustness remain the same when interchanging
\pi^{\rm miss,dep}_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(x,{\mathbf{z}})
from the missing covariate problem with
\pi^{\rm cens,dep}_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(x,{\mathbf{z}})
from the right-censored covariate problem, and
R
with
\Delta
. Let the probabilities
\pi^{\rm miss,dep}_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\xi}})=f_{R|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(r=1,x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\xi}})
be indexed by parameter
{\boldsymbol{\xi}}
. When
f_{R|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}
is assumed known, the asymptotic normality of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\rm IPW}
remains the same as for independent covariate missingness, but instead of probabilities defined by
f_{R|{\mathbf{Z}}}
, we use
f_{R|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}
. When
f_{R|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}
is unknown, a consistent and asymptotically normal estimator needs to be obtained first. As described in Section
5
, obtaining this estimator is particularly difficult for the missing covariate problem, as that requires more unverifiable assumptions to satisfy identifiability than are needed for the dependent right-censored covariate problem. If identifiability is established, then we can follow a similar argument as in the right-censored covariate problem to derive the asymptotic distribution of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\rm IPW}
by interchanging the influence functions of the nuisance parameters with those from the independent missingness case.

3.

MLE:

Under dependent covariate missingness, the MLE estimating equations use
f_{X|R,{\mathbf{Z}}}
as opposed to
f_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}
, and the domain of integration is
{\cal D}(x)
. By (i) interchanging the roles of
f_{X|R,{\mathbf{Z}}}
with
f_{X|C,{\mathbf{Z}}}
from the right-censored covariate problem, and (ii) defining the domain of integration of
X
(i.e.,
{\cal D}(x)
), the proofs of consistency and robustness remain the same as those in the right-censored covariate problem. That is, the MLE is robust to
f_{R|{\mathbf{Z}}}
and
f_{{\mathbf{Z}}}
, but not
f_{X|R,{\mathbf{Z}}}
. When assuming that
f_{X|R,{\mathbf{Z}}}
is known, the proof of asymptotic normality distribution of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\rm MLE}
remains the same as that for independent covariate missingness using
f_{X|R,{\mathbf{Z}}}
instead of
f_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}
. When
f_{X|R,{\mathbf{Z}}}
is unknown, we run into a similar issue as we did for the IPW estimator. That is, a consistent and asymptotically normal estimator for the nuisance distribution needs to be obtained first, but doing so will involve more unverifiable assumptions than those needed for dependent covariate right-censoring. If identifiability is established, then a similar argument as that for independent covariate missingness is made to define the asymptotic distribution of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\rm MLE}
.

4.

ACC estimator:

The proofs of robustness and asymptotic normality of the ACC estimator under dependent covariate missingness can be found in the supplementary material of
Bartlett et al. (2014a)
. In that paper, the authors use semi-parametric theory to define orthogonal spaces for the the parameters governing the distributions of the the mean form of the restricted mean model (i.e.,
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}
in our case) and those from the probability of observing
X
given
(Y,{\mathbf{Z}})
. By interchanging the roles of our score equation in our paper with the restricted mean model in their paper, the proof remains the same.

5.

MACC estimator:

Under dependent covariate missingness, the MACC estimator uses the probabilities
\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}
as opposed to
\pi_{{\mathbf{Z}}}
. Let the probabilities
\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\xi}})=f_{R|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(r=1,x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\xi}})
be indexed by parameter
{\boldsymbol{\xi}}
. By interchanging the roles of
(W,\Delta)
with
(XR,R)
, and
\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}^{\rm miss,dep}(x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\xi}})
with
\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}^{\rm cens,dep}(x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}})
, the proofs of consistency and robustness remain the same as those for the right-censored covariate problem. Additionally, the asymptotic normality of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\rm MACC}
when
f_{R|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}
is assumed known remains the same as that for the right-censored covariate problem when
f_{C|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}
is assumed known. When
f_{R|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}
is unknown, a consistent and asymptotically normal estimator needs to be obtained first. Identifiability requires more unverifiable assumptions (Section
5
), but if identifiability is established, then a similar argument to the ACC estimator is made to define the asymptotic distribution of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{\rm MACC;{\boldsymbol{\xi}}}
, i.e., by interchanging the influence functions of the nuisance parameters. When the MACC estimator uses
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}
, the same considerations apply.

6.

AIPW estimator:

The proofs of robustness and asymptotic normality of the AIPW estimator under dependent covariate missingness can be found in
Rotnitzky & Robins (1997a)
. In that paper, the authors use semi-parametric theory to define orthogonal spaces for the parameters governing the distributions of the the mean form of the restricted mean model (i.e.,
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}
in our case) and those from the probability of observing
X
given the observed data (i.e.,
{\boldsymbol{\xi}}
in our case). By interchanging the roles of our score equation with their restricted mean model, the proof remains the same. When the MACC uses
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}_{\rm MACC}
, the same considerations apply.

S.4

Proof of Theorem
2

By definition, the MLE achieves optimal efficiency when correctly specified, making it the most efficient estimator when compared to the IPW, ACC, MACC, and AIPW estimators. The ACC and MACC estimators are more statistically efficient than the CC estimator as they were constructed to satisfy the efficiency condition in equation (
2
). Similarly, the AIPW estimator is more statistically efficient than the IPW estimator by construction.

In the subsequent sections, we first present preliminary results in the form of corollaries, then we use these to show that the CC estimator is more efficient than the IPW (Section
S.4.2
) and AIPW (Section
S.4.3
) estimators. We show this result by comparing the diagonal elements of the covariance matrix for the CC, IPW, and AIPW estimators, which represent the variances of each individual component of
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}
. We show that the individual variance components of the CC estimator are equal to or smaller than those of the IPW and AIPW estimators. While we show that the diagonal elements of the CC estimator are equally or smaller than those provided by the IPW and AIPW estimators, it may be the case that the conditional mean of the outcome
Y
given the set of covariate values
(X=x,{\mathbf{Z}}={\mathbf{z}})
using the IPW and AIPW estimators may be smaller than that of the CC estimator since in our proof of higher efficiency of the CC estimator, we do not establish the relationship between the off-diagonal elements of the covariance matrix.

S.4.1

Supporting corollaries

(C1)

Proof
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}=-{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm CC}

To show that
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}=-{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm CC}
, we must show that
E\{\Delta\partial{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})/\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\}=

-E\{\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{\otimes 2}\}
. Given that
E\{\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\}={\mathbf{0}}
then it follows that

\displaystyle{\mathbf{0}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\int\delta{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}({y},w,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})f_{Y,W,\Delta,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,w,\delta,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})dydwd\delta d{\mathbf{z}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\delta{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}({y},w,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\biggr\{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}f_{Y,W,\Delta,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,w,\delta,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\biggr\}+

\displaystyle\quad\quad\biggr\{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\delta{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}({y},w,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\biggr\}f_{Y,W,\Delta,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,w,\delta,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\biggr]dydwd\delta d{\mathbf{z}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\delta{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}({y},w,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\biggr\{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}f_{Y,W,\Delta,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,w,\delta,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\biggr\}dydwd\delta d{\mathbf{z}}+E\{\Delta\partial{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})/\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\}.

The partial derivative of the integral is equal to

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}f_{Y,W,\Delta,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,w,\delta,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\biggr\{\int_{x\leq C}f_{Y,X,C,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,w,c,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})dc\biggr\}^{\delta}\biggr\{\int_{c<X}f_{Y,X,C,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,x,w,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})dx\biggr\}^{1-\delta}

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\biggr\{f_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,w,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\int_{x\leq C}f_{X,C,{\mathbf{Z}}}(w,c,{\mathbf{z}})dc\biggr\}^{\delta}\biggr\{\int_{c<X}f_{Y,X,C,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,x,w,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})dx\biggr\}^{1-\delta}

\displaystyle=\biggr\{f_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,w,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\int_{x\leq C}f_{X,C,{\mathbf{Z}}}(w,c,{\mathbf{z}})dc\biggr\}^{\delta}(1-\delta)\biggr\{\int_{c<X}f_{Y,X,C,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,x,w,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})dx\biggr\}^{-\delta}

\displaystyle\times\biggr\{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\int_{c<X}f_{Y,X,C,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,x,w,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})dx\biggr\}+\biggr\{\int_{c<X}f_{Y,X,C,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,x,w,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})dx\biggr\}^{1-\delta}

\displaystyle\times\delta\biggr\{f_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,w,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\int_{x\leq C}f_{X,C,{\mathbf{Z}}}(w,c,{\mathbf{z}})dc\biggr\}^{\delta-1}\biggr\{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}f_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,w,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\int_{x\leq C}f_{X,C,{\mathbf{Z}}}(w,c,{\mathbf{z}})dc\biggr\}.

The integral with respect to
\delta=0
is equal to
{\mathbf{0}}
and the integral with respect to
\delta=1
leads to

\displaystyle\int\delta{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}({y},w,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\biggr\{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}f_{Y,W,\Delta,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,w,\delta,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\biggr\}dydwd\delta d{\mathbf{z}}

\displaystyle=\int{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}({y},x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}f_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\int_{x\leq C}f_{X,C,{\mathbf{Z}}}(w,c,{\mathbf{z}})dcdydxd{\mathbf{z}}

\displaystyle=\int I(X\leq C){\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}({y},x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{\otimes 2}f_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})f_{X,C,{\mathbf{Z}}}(w,c,{\mathbf{z}})dcdydxd{\mathbf{z}}

\displaystyle=E\{\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{\otimes 2}\}

where the second-to-last line follows since
\partial f_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})/\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}={\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}({y},x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})f_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
. It follows that

\displaystyle{\mathbf{0}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}\int\delta{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}({y},w,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})f_{Y,W,\Delta,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,w,\delta,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})dydwd\delta d{\mathbf{z}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\{\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{\otimes 2}\}+E\{\Delta\partial{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})/\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\}.

Therefore,
E\{\Delta\partial{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})/\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}\}=-E\{\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{\otimes 2}\}
, and
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}=-{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm CC}
.

(C2)

Proof
E\{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm IPW}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0}){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}\}=-{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}

To prove consistency, we showed
E\{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm IPW}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})\}=E\{\delta{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})/\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})\}={\mathbf{0}}
. Using a similar argument as in Corollary
(C1)
, it follows that

\displaystyle{\mathbf{0}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}}E\{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm IPW}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}}\frac{\delta{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}({y},w,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(w,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}f_{Y,W,\Delta,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,w,\delta,{\mathbf{z}})dydwd\delta d{\mathbf{z}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int I(X\leq C)\frac{{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}({y},x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}I(X\leq C){\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}({y},x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}f_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})f_{X,C,{\mathbf{Z}}}(w,c,{\mathbf{z}})dcdydxd{\mathbf{z}}

\displaystyle+E\biggr\{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}}\frac{\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}\biggr\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\biggr\{\frac{\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}\delta{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}\biggr\}+E\biggr\{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}}\frac{\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}\biggr\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm IPW}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0}){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}\}+E\biggr\{\frac{\partial}{\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}}\frac{\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}\biggr\}.

Since
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}=E\left\{\partial/\partial{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{T}\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})/\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})\right\}
, it follows that

\displaystyle E\{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm IPW}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0}){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}\}=-{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}.

(C3)

Proof
E\{{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}^{*}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*}){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}\}={\mathbf{0}}

Let
{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}^{*}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*})={\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm IPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*})
where
{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*})=-[E\{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})/\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}\}]^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})
and
{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm IPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}=E\{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm IPW}({\mathbf{O}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})/\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}\}
. Then,

\displaystyle E\{{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}^{*}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*}){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}\}

\displaystyle=E_{Y,W,\Delta,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm IPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}(W,\Delta,{\mathbf{Z}})\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}\}

\displaystyle={\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm IPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}E_{Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}(X,\Delta=1,{\mathbf{Z}})\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0}){\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}\}

\displaystyle={\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm IPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}E_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}[{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}(X,\Delta=1,{\mathbf{Z}})\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})E_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}\}].

The second line follows since
{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm IPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}
is a fixed matrix, and the last line follows since
E_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(y,x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}\}={\mathbf{0}}
. In the case of the consistent and asymptotically normal estimator of
\alpha
discussed in Section
S.2.1
, it follows that

\displaystyle E\{{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}^{*}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*}){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}\}

\displaystyle=-{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm IPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}E_{Y,W,\Delta,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}{\mathbf{S}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}(W,\Delta,{\mathbf{Z}};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$})\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}\}

\displaystyle=-{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm IPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}\ E_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}\biggr\{\frac{\int_{x\leq C}\{\frac{\partial}{\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}}\hbox{log}f_{C,X|{\mathbf{Z}}}(c,x,{\mathbf{z}};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$})\}f_{C,X|{\mathbf{Z}}}(c,x,{\mathbf{z}};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$})dc}{\int_{x\leq C}f_{C,X|{\mathbf{Z}}}(c,x,{\mathbf{z}};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$})dc}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times E_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{Z}}){\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\}^{T}\biggr\}

\displaystyle=-{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm IPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}E_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left[{\mathbf{S}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}(X,\Delta=1,{\mathbf{Z}})E_{Y|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{Z}}){\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\}^{T}\right]

\displaystyle=-{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm IPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}\ E_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left[{\mathbf{S}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}(X,\Delta=1,{\mathbf{Z}})\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{Z}})\times{\mathbf{0}}^{T}\right]={\mathbf{0}}.

(C4)

Proof that the diagonal elements of
E[\{1-\Delta/\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW,eff}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}]
are non-negative.

It follows that

\displaystyle E_{Y,W,\Delta,{\mathbf{Z}}}[\{1-\Delta/\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW,eff}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}]
