\displaystyle=E_{Y,W,\Delta,{\mathbf{Z}}}[\{1-\delta/\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(w,{\mathbf{z}})\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW,eff}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})\delta{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(y,w,{\mathbf{z}})^{T}]

\displaystyle=E_{Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}}}[\{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})-1\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW,eff}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}){\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}].

The resulting expression is equal to

\displaystyle E_{Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}}}[\{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})-1\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW,eff}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}){\mathbf{S}}_{\boldsymbol{\theta}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}]

\displaystyle=E_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\biggr(\frac{1}{E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{1/\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})-1\}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\times E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}[\{1/\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})-1\}{\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\times E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}[\{1-\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})\}{\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}]\biggr)

Assuming that
0<\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(x,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})<1
, then
E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{1/\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})\}>1
because the expectation is with respect to a function whose range is strictly greater than 1. Therefore, we conclude that the denominator will lead to a positive number. We prove that the diagonal elements of the numerator are non-negative by contradiction by assuming that the diagonal elements of the numerator are strictly less than zero. We achieve this result if the two expectation terms are opposite signs. Suppose that
E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}[\{1/\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})-1\}{\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}(y,X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})]
is a positive and non-zero vector, and
E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}[\{1-\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})\}{\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}(y,X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})]
is a negative and non-zero vector. This assumption implies that

\displaystyle{\mathbf{0}}

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}[\{1/\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})-1\}{\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}(y,X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{{\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}(y,X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})/\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})\}-E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{{\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}(y,X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\};

\displaystyle{\mathbf{0}}

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}[\{1-\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})\}{\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}(y,X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{{\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}(y,X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\}-E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0}){\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}(y,X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\}.

These two results imply that for all
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}
,

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{c}E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{{\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}(y,X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})/\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})\}\\
E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0}){\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}(y,X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\}.\end{array}

\displaystyle>

\displaystyle E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{{\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}(y,X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\}

This statement is a contradiction because only one statement can be correct. Either (i)
E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{{\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}(y,X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})/\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})\}>E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{{\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}(y,X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\}

or (ii)

E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0}){\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}(y,X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\}>E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}\{{\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}(y,X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\}
, but not both. Therefore, we conclude that the product of
E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}[\{1/\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})-1\}{\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}(y,X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})]
and
E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}[\{1-\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})\}{\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}(y,X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})]
is a non-negative vector. By proving that this product is in the non-negative domain, we prove that the diagonal elements of
E[\{1-\Delta/\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW,eff}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}]
are non-negative.

(C5)

Proof that the diagonal elements of
{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}_{\rm CC}
and
{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}_{\rm IPW}
are of equivalent signs.

In Corollary
(C1)
, we showed that
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}=-{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm CC}
, therefore,
{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}_{\rm CC}=-{\mathbf{B}}^{-1}_{\rm CC}
. It follows that the diagonal elements of
{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}_{\rm CC}
are negative since (i) the diagonal elements of
{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm CC}
are strictly positive due to the property of the sandwich variance estimator that
\hbox{diag}\{{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}_{\rm CC}{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm CC}{\mathbf{A}}^{-T}_{\rm CC}\}>{\mathbf{0}}
and (ii) inverting the
{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm CC}
matrix does not change the sign of the diagonal elements.

Now, it remains to be shown that the diagonal elements of
{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}_{\rm IPW}
are non-negative. We will begin by showing the diagonal elements of
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}
are non-negative. In Corollary
(C2)
, we showed that
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}=-E\{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm IPW}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0}){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}\}
. It follows that

\displaystyle{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-E\{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm IPW}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0}){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-E\left\{\frac{\Delta{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-E_{Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left\{\frac{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0}){\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})}{\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})}{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-E_{Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left\{{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{\otimes 2}\right\}.

Since
E_{Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}}}\left\{{\mathbf{S}}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}^{F}(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{\otimes 2}\right\}
is the expectation of a matrix with non-negative diagonal values, then the resulting expectation in non-negative and we conclude that the diagonal values of
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}
are less than zero. Finally, we conclude that the diagonal values of
{\mathbf{A}}^{-1}_{\rm IPW}
are strictly negative since inverting the
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}
matrix does not change the sign of the diagonal elements.

S.4.2

Efficiency comparison of the CC and IPW estimators

We show that the CC estimator is as or more efficient than the IPW estimator whether the probabilities are known or estimated.

1.

Known probabilities:

To prove that the CC estimator is as or more efficient than the IPW estimator it is sufficient to show that
\hbox{diag}\{{\rm Var}({{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{\rm IPW})\}\geq\hbox{diag}\{{\rm Var}({{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{\rm CC})\}
. The variances of the CC and IPW estimators are equal to

\displaystyle{\rm Var}({{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{\rm CC})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm Var}\{{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}})\},{\rm\ where}\ {\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}})=-{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}^{-1}{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm CC}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}^{-T},{\rm\ where}\ {\mathbf{B}}_{\rm CC}=E\{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{\otimes 2}\};

\displaystyle{\rm Var}({{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{\rm IPW})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm Var}\{{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\rm IPW}({\mathbf{O}})\},{\rm\ where}\ {\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\rm IPW}({\mathbf{O}})=-{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm IPW}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}^{-1}{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm IPW}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}^{-T};{\rm\ where}\ {\mathbf{B}}_{\rm IPW}=E\{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm IPW}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})^{\otimes 2}\}.

Using the property that the expectation of a non-negative function is non-negative, it follows that

\hbox{diag}\left(E[\{{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}})-{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\rm IPW}({\mathbf{O}})\}^{\otimes 2}]\right)\geq{\mathbf{0}}
. This inequality indicates that each individual cell component of the matrix is equal to or greater than zero. We have that

\displaystyle\hbox{diag}\left(E[\{{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}})-{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\rm IPW}({\mathbf{O}})\}^{\otimes 2}]\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hbox{diag}\biggr[{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}^{-1}{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm CC}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}^{-T}+{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}^{-1}{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm IPW}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}^{-T}

\displaystyle-{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}^{-1}E\{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm IPW}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})^{T}\}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}^{-T}

\displaystyle-{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}^{-1}E\{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm IPW}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0}){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}\}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}^{-T}\biggr].

In Corollaries
(C1)
and
(C2)
, respectively, we show that
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}=-{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm CC}
and

E\{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm IPW}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0}){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}\}=-{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}
. From these results, it follows that

\displaystyle\hbox{diag}\left[E\{({\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\rm CC}-{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\rm IPW})^{\otimes 2}\}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hbox{diag}\left(-{\mathbf{A}}^{-T}_{\rm CC}+{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}^{-1}{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm IPW}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}^{-T}+{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}^{-1}+{\mathbf{A}}^{-T}_{\rm CC}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hbox{diag}\left({\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}^{-1}{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm IPW}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}^{-T}+{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\hbox{diag}\left({\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}^{-1}{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm IPW}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}^{-T}-{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}^{-1}{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm CC}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}^{-T}\right),

where the second-to-last line follows since
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}=-{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm CC}
and
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}^{-1}=-{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}^{-1}{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm CC}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}^{-T}
. Lastly,
since the expression is greater than or equal to
{\mathbf{0}}
, we have that

\displaystyle\hbox{diag}\left({\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}^{-1}{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm IPW}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}^{-T}\right)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\hbox{diag}\left({\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}^{-1}{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm CC}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}^{-T}\right),

which means
\hbox{diag}\left\{{\rm Var}({{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{\rm IPW})\right\}\geq\hbox{diag}\left\{{\rm Var}({{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{\rm CC})\right\}
. We conclude that the CC estimator is as or more efficient than the IPW estimator when
f_{C|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}
is known. By interchanging the roles of
f_{C|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}
with
f_{C|{\mathbf{Z}}}
, the same result follows under independent covariate right-censoring. This result continues to hold for independent and dependent covariate missingness.

2.

Unknown probabilities:

When the probabilities are unknown, we need to estimate the set of parameters
{\boldsymbol{\eta}}
that index the
f_{C|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}
distribution. In this case, we consider the influence function

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\rm IPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}})

\displaystyle=-{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}^{-1}\{({\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm IPW}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})

\displaystyle\quad-E\{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm IPW}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})/\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}\}[E\{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})/\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}\}]^{-1}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0}))\}

\displaystyle=-{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}^{-1}\{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm IPW}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})+{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm IPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*})\},

where
{\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm IPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}=E\{\partial{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm IPW}({\mathbf{O}}_{i};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})/\partial\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}\}
is a fixed matrix. Without loss of generality, let
{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}^{*}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*})={\mathbf{A}}^{*}_{\rm IPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*})
. It follows that

\displaystyle{\rm Var}({{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{\rm IPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm Var}({\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\rm IPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}})={\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}^{-1}{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm IPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}^{-T},

where
{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm IPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}=E[\{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm IPW}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})+{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}^{*}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*})\}^{\otimes 2}]
. Applying a similar argument as that when
f_{C|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}
was known,

\displaystyle\hbox{diag}\left(E[\{{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}})-{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\rm IPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}})\}^{\otimes 2}\}]\right)

\displaystyle=\hbox{diag}\biggr[{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}^{-1}{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm CC}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}^{-T}+{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}^{-1}{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm IPW}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}^{-T}

\displaystyle-{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}^{-1}E\{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm IPW}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})^{T}\}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}^{-T}-{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}^{-1}E\{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm IPW}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0}){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}\}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}^{-T}

\displaystyle-{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}^{-1}E\{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}){\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}^{*}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*})^{T}\}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}^{-T}-{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}^{-1}E\{{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}^{*}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*}){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}\}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}^{-T}\biggr].

As shown in Corollary
(C3)
,
E\{{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}^{*}_{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}}^{*}){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}\}={\mathbf{0}}
, so the expression may be reduced to

\displaystyle\hbox{diag}\left(E[\{{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}})-{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\rm IPW;\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}}({\mathbf{O}})\}^{\otimes 2}\}]\right)

\displaystyle=\hbox{diag}\biggr[{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}^{-1}{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm CC}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}^{-T}+{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}^{-1}{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm IPW}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}^{-T}-{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}^{-1}E\{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm IPW}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0})^{T}\}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}^{-T}

\displaystyle\quad-{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}^{-1}E\{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm IPW}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{0}){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}\}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}^{-T}\biggr].

The remainder of the proof remains the same as that used when the probabilities were known. We conclude that the CC estimator is as or more efficient than the IPW estimator when the probabilities are unknown. By interchanging the roles of
f_{C|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}
with
f_{C|{\mathbf{Z}}}
, as well as their corresponding influence functions, the same result follows under independent covariate right-censoring. A similar argument shows that this result holds for independent and dependent covariate missingness.

S.4.3

Efficiency comparison of the CC and AIPW estimators

Here we show that the CC estimator is as or more efficient than the AIPW estimator, regardless of whether the probabilities are known or estimated. We will compare the CC estimator to the AIPW estimator with the augmentation term
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW,eff}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
. That is,

\displaystyle{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW,eff}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}[\{1-1/\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})\}{\mathbf{S}}^{F}_{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}(y,X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})]}{E_{X|Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}[\{1/\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(X,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})-1\}]}.

1.

Known probabilities:

To prove that the CC estimator is as or more efficient than the AIPW estimator it is sufficient to show that
\hbox{diag}\left\{{\rm Var}({{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{\rm AIPW})\right\}\geq\left\{{\rm Var}({{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{\rm CC})\right\}
. The variance of the CC and AIPW estimators is equal to

\displaystyle{\rm Var}({{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{\rm CC})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm Var}\{{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}})\},{\rm\ where}\ {\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}})=-{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}^{-1}{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm CC}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}^{-T},{\rm\ where}\ {\mathbf{B}}_{\rm CC}=E\{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{\otimes 2}\};

\displaystyle{\rm Var}({{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{\rm AIPW})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm Var}\{{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\rm AIPW}({\mathbf{O}})\},{\rm\ where}\ {\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\rm AIPW}({\mathbf{O}})=-{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm AIPW}{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm AIPW}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm AIPW}^{-1}{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm AIPW}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm AIPW}^{-T};{\rm\ where}\ {\mathbf{B}}_{\rm AIPW}=E\{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm AIPW}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})^{\otimes 2}\}.

From the proofs of consistency and asymptotic normality, we know that
{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm AIPW}={\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}
. Similar to the proof for the CC and IPW estimators, we use the fact that
\hbox{diag}\left(E[\{{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}})-{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\rm AIPW}({\mathbf{O}})\}^{\otimes 2}]\right)\geq{\mathbf{0}}
. It follows that

\displaystyle\hbox{diag}\left(E[\{{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}})-{\boldsymbol{\Upsilon}}_{\rm AIPW}({\mathbf{O}})\}^{\otimes 2}]\right)

\displaystyle=\hbox{diag}\bigr[{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}^{-1}{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm CC}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}^{-T}+{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm AIPW}^{-1}{\mathbf{B}}_{\rm AIPW}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm AIPW}^{-T}-{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}^{-1}E\{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm AIPW}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})^{T}\}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm AIPW}^{-T}

\displaystyle\quad-{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm AIPW}^{-1}E\{{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm AIPW}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0},{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0}){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}\}{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}^{-T}\bigr]

\displaystyle=\hbox{diag}\bigr(-{\rm Var}({{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{\rm CC})+{\rm Var}({{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{\rm AIPW})

\displaystyle\quad-{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}^{-1}E[{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\{1-\Delta/\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}]{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}^{-T}

\displaystyle\quad-{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}^{-1}E[\{1-\Delta/\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}]{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}^{-T}\bigr).

In
(C4)
, it is shown that
\hbox{diag}\left(E[\{1-\Delta/\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}]\right)\geq{\mathbf{0}}
, and in
(C5)
, it is shown that
\hbox{diag}({\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}^{-1})
and
\hbox{diag}({\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}^{-1})
are of equivalent sign. Therefore, it follows that

\displaystyle\hbox{diag}\left\{{\rm Var}({{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{\rm AIPW})\right\}

\displaystyle\geq\hbox{diag}\bigr({\rm Var}({{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{\rm CC})+{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}^{-1}E[{\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}},{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})\{1-\Delta/\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}]{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}^{-T}

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm IPW}^{-1}E[\{1-\Delta/\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(W,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\eta}}_{0})\}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW}(Y,{\mathbf{Z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}){\boldsymbol{\Phi}}_{\rm CC}({\mathbf{O}},{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})^{T}]{\mathbf{A}}_{\rm CC}^{-T}\bigr)

\displaystyle\geq\hbox{diag}\left\{{\rm Var}({{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{\rm CC})\right\}.

We conclude that the CC estimator is as or more efficient than the AIPW estimator when
f_{C|X,{\mathbf{Z}}}
is known. By interchanging the roles of
f_{C|X,Z}
with
f_{C|Z}
, the same result follows under independent covariate right-censoring. A similar argument can be applied as that for the missing covariate problem.

2.

Unknown weights: In our derivations for the AIPW estimator, we did not provide the form of
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm AIPW,eff}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})
for unknown weights. Therefore, the proof holds only for known weights.

S.5

More details of the simulation study

S.5.1

Data generation

In all
N=3,000
simulations, we generated data with a sample size of
n=1,000
and a censoring rate of
50\%
. Simulations were repeated using a smaller sample size of
n=100
to validate results. Data were generated from the regression model in Equation (
1
), where
m(X,Z;{\boldsymbol{\theta}})=\beta_{0}+\beta_{AX}(A-X)+\beta_{Z}Z
,
\epsilon\sim\hbox{Normal}(0,1)
, and
A\sim\hbox{Normal}(0,1)
. The true parameter values were
\beta_{0}=1
,
\beta_{AX}=3
, and
\beta_{Z}=2
. We used
(A-X)
as opposed to
X

alone to mimic the case when time to diagnosis is of interest; here,
A
is the current age and
X
is the age of diagnosis, so
A-X
is the time to diagnosis. We generated data
(X,C,Z)
from a normal distribution as follows:

\displaystyle(X,C,Z)^{{}^{\rm T}}\sim\hbox{Normal}\left\{{\boldsymbol{\mu}}=\begin{bmatrix}0\\
0\\
0\end{bmatrix},{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}=\begin{bmatrix}\sigma_{X}^{2}=1&-&-\\
\sigma_{XC}&\sigma_{C}^{2}=4&-\\
\sigma_{XZ}=0.50&\sigma_{CZ}=0.50&\sigma_{Z}^{2}=1\end{bmatrix}\right\}.

To guarantee independent covariate right-censoring, we set
\sigma_{XC}=0.25
, so that the covariance of
(X,C)
conditional on
Z
equals 0. To guarantee dependent covariate right-censoring, we set
\sigma_{XC}=0.60
, so that the covariance of
(X,C)
conditional on
Z
does not equal 0. We generated
N=1,000
datasets following the above procedure and then estimated
(\beta_{0},\beta_{AX},\beta_{Z})
. Next, we explain what the nuisance parameters of the distribution
f_{C,X|Z}
are based on our trivariate normal distribution, and how these are used to calculate the probabilities
\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,z)
and
\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(x,{\mathbf{z}})
under independent and dependent covariate right-censoring.

S.5.1.1

Independent covariate right-censoring

The distribution of
C
conditional on
Z
is given by

\displaystyle\mu_{C|Z}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mu_{C}+(\sigma_{CZ}/\sigma_{Z}^{2})*(z_{i}-\mu_{Z})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha_{10}+\alpha_{11}z_{i};

\displaystyle\sigma_{C|Z}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma_{C}^{2}-\sigma_{CZ}^{2}/\sigma_{Z}^{2}.

Similarly, the distribution of
X
conditional on
Z
is given by

\displaystyle\mu_{X|Z}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mu_{X}+(\sigma_{XZ}/\sigma_{Z}^{2})*(z_{i}-\mu_{Z})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha_{20}+\alpha_{21}z_{i};

\displaystyle\sigma_{X|Z}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma_{X}^{2}-\sigma_{XZ}^{2}/\sigma_{Z}^{2}.

Under our simulation settings,
\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{1}=(\alpha_{10},\alpha_{11},\sigma_{C|Z}^{2})^{T}=(0,0.5,3.75)^{T}
and
\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{2}=(\alpha_{20},\alpha_{21},\sigma_{X|Z}^{2})^{T}=(0,0.5,0.75)^{T}
. The probability of observing
X
is defined by

\displaystyle\pi_{X,Z}(x_{i},z_{i})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\hbox{pr}(x_{i}\leq C|Z=z_{i})=\int_{x_{i}<c}f_{C|Z}(c,z)dc

\displaystyle\pi_{Y,Z}(y_{i},z_{i})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int\pi_{X,Z}(x,z_{i})f_{X|Y,Z}(x,y,z)dx

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\int\pi_{X,Z}(x,z_{i})f_{Y|X,Z}(y_{i},x,z_{i};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})f_{X|Z}(x,z_{i})dx}{\int f_{Y|X,Z}(y_{i},x,z_{i};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})f_{X|Z}(x,z_{i})dx}.

The probability
\pi_{X,Z}(x_{i},z_{i})
corresponds to the upper-tail of the cumulative distribution function (CDF) of a conditional normal distribution governed by parameters
\mu_{C|Z}
and
\sigma_{C|Z}^{2}
, and evaluated at
z_{i}
. The probability
\pi_{Y,Z}(y_{i},z_{i})
is calculated by computing the ratio of integrals. Since all variables are normally distributed, this calculation narrows down to a straightforward numeral integration.

S.5.1.2

Dependent covariate right-censoring

Under dependent covariate right-censoring, the distribution of
C
conditional on
X
and
Z
is
also governed by a mean and variance. The mean is equal to

\displaystyle\mu_{C|X,Z}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mu_{C}+\frac{(\sigma_{XC}\sigma_{Z}^{2}-\sigma_{CZ}\sigma_{XZ})(x_{i}-\mu_{X})+(\sigma_{CZ}\sigma_{X}^{2}-\sigma_{XC}*\sigma_{XZ})(z_{i}-\mu_{Z})}{\sigma_{X}^{2}\sigma_{Z}^{2}-\sigma_{XZ}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha_{30}+\alpha_{31}x_{i}+\alpha_{32}z_{i}

such that

\displaystyle\alpha_{30}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mu_{C}-\frac{(\sigma_{XC}\sigma_{Z}^{2}-\sigma_{CZ}\sigma_{XZ})\mu_{X}}{\sigma_{X}^{2}\sigma_{Z}^{2}-\sigma_{XZ}^{2}}-\frac{(\sigma_{CZ}\sigma_{X}^{2}-\sigma_{XC}*\sigma_{XZ})\mu_{Z}}{\sigma_{X}^{2}\sigma_{Z}^{2}-\sigma_{XZ}^{2}};

\displaystyle\alpha_{31}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(\sigma_{XC}\sigma_{Z}^{2}-\sigma_{CZ}\sigma_{XZ})}{\sigma_{X}^{2}\sigma_{Z}^{2}-\sigma_{XZ}^{2}};\alpha_{32}=\frac{(\sigma_{CZ}\sigma_{X}^{2}-\sigma_{XC}\sigma_{XZ})}{\sigma_{X}^{2}\sigma_{Z}^{2}-\sigma_{XZ}^{2}}.

The variance is equal to

\displaystyle\sigma_{C|X,Z}^{2}=\sigma_{C}^{2}-\frac{(\sigma_{XC}\sigma_{Z}^{2}-\sigma_{CZ}\sigma_{XZ})\sigma_{XC}+(\sigma_{CZ}\sigma_{X}^{2}-\sigma_{XC}\sigma_{XZ})\sigma_{CZ}}{\sigma_{X}^{2}\sigma_{Z}^{2}-\sigma_{XZ}^{2}}.

Under our simulation settings, the parameters are equal to
\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{1}=(\alpha_{30},\alpha_{31},\alpha_{32},\sigma_{C|X,Z}^{2})=(0,0.35/0.75,0.2/0.75,299/75)
. The distribution of
X
conditional on
Z
is the same as for independent covariate censoring (i.e., the same
\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}_{2}
). The probability of observing
X
is now defined as

\displaystyle\pi_{X,Z}(x_{i},z_{i})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\hbox{pr}(x_{i}\leq C|X=x_{i},Z=z_{i})=\int_{x<c}f_{C|X,Z}(c,x,{\mathbf{z}})dc

\displaystyle\pi_{Y,Z}(y_{i},z_{i})

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\int\pi_{X,Z}(x,z_{i})f_{X|Y,Z}(x,y,z)dx;

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\int\pi_{X,Z}(x,z_{i})f_{Y|X,Z}(y_{i},x,z_{i};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})f_{X|Z}(x,z_{i})dx}{\int f_{Y|X,Z}(y_{i},x,z_{i};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})f_{X|Z}(x,z_{i})dx}.

These probabilities are then computed similarly as for independent covariate right-censoring.

We have outlined the form of the nuisance parameters governing
f_{X,C|{\mathbf{Z}}}
. Now, we describe how these nuisance parameters were specified in our simulation study. We employed two approaches: a mechanistic approach, where the nuisance parameters were fully specified using the true values, and an empirical approach, where the nuisance parameters were estimated from the data.

S.5.2

Nuisance parameters are specified

When the nuisance parameters were specified, the estimators were implemented as follows:

1.

IPW estimator. Two variations for
\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(w,{\mathbf{z}})
were considered: (i) correctly specified and generated from the multivariate normal distribution; and (ii) incorrectly specified and generated from uniform(0.1, 0.9). We also considered the correct specification of
\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}
to show that this choice will lead to an inconsistent estimator.

2.

MLE. Two variations for
f_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}
(
f_{X|C,{\mathbf{Z}}}
under dependent censoring) were considered: (i) correctly specified from the trivariate normal distribution; and (ii) incorrectly specified as a normal distribution with mean
-2
and variance 1.

3.

ACC, MACC, and AIPW estimators. Four cases were considered: (i)
\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,{\mathbf{z}})
or
\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(w,{\mathbf{z}})
and
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})
correctly specified; (ii) only
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})
correctly specified; (iii) only
\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,{\mathbf{z}})
or
\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(w,{\mathbf{z}})
correctly specified; and (iv)
\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,{\mathbf{z}})
or
\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(w,{\mathbf{z}})
and
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})
incorrectly specified. Incorrectly specified
\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,{\mathbf{z}})
or
\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(w,{\mathbf{z}})
was generated from uniform(0.1, 0.9), and the incorrect
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})
was chosen to be
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm close}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})
. Correctly specified
\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,{\mathbf{z}})
or
\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(w,{\mathbf{z}})
was calculated from the trivariate normal distribution, and correct
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})
was calculated as in Tables
1
and
3
.

4.

ACC, MACC, and AIPW with
{\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}
estimators. The augmented component was chosen to be
{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm updated}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})={\boldsymbol{\Lambda}}{\boldsymbol{\Psi}}_{\rm close}(y,{\mathbf{z}};{\boldsymbol{\theta}})
. Two variations for the
\pi_{Y,{\mathbf{Z}}}(y,{\mathbf{z}})
or
\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(w,{\mathbf{z}})
were considered: (i) correctly specified and generated from the trivariate normal distribution; and (ii) incorrectly specified and generated from uniform(0.1, 0.9).

We also implemented the oracle, naive, and complete case estimators. The oracle estimator estimates
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
using the full but unobserved data
(Y,X,{\mathbf{Z}})
. Constructing the oracle estimator is not possible in practice since
X
is right-censored, but it gives us a gold standard to assess performance. In contrast, the naive estimator, which uses all observations and naively treats
W
as
X
in the estimation of
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
, was used to showcase the worst-case scenario. Finally, the complete case estimator was used to showcase the performance of an estimator that only includes complete observations.

S.5.3

Nuisance distributions are estimated

When the nuisance parameters were estimated, we used a conditional bivariate normal distribution and the parameters governing this distribution
\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}^{T}=(\mu_{X|Z},\mu_{C|Z},\sigma_{X|Z},\sigma_{C|Z})
were estimated. All estimators were considered for the independent covariate right-censoring case and only correctly specified scenarios were evaluated: the weights
\pi_{X,{\mathbf{Z}}}(w,{\mathbf{z}})
were calculated using
f_{C|{\mathbf{Z}}}(c,{\mathbf{z}};\widehat{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}})
, and
f_{X|{\mathbf{Z}}}(x,{\mathbf{z}};\widehat{\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$}})
was used for the augmented estimators and the MLE. For dependent censoring,
f_{X,C|{\mathbf{Z}}}
is not always identifiable; therefore only estimators that do not require this distribution were evaluated: oracle, naive, complete case, and ACC. To showcase that the ACC estimator in the dependent missing problem can be used for the right-censored covariate problem, we modified the simulation study from
Bartlett et al. (2014a)
by letting
C=X-beta\{\exp(Z),1\}
when
X
was not observed.

We computed all simulations using the
geex
package in R
(
Saul & Hudgens, 2020a
)
. Then, we evaluated all estimators by computing the mean estimate of
{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
(i.e.,
N^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{i}
) and its percent bias (i.e.,
N^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\theta}}}_{i}-{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0})/{\boldsymbol{\theta}}_{0}
); the empirical standard deviation of
\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
across all simulations; and the empirical mean of the estimated standard errors (i.e.,
N^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\widehat{\rm SE}_{i}
). Estimated standard errors were computed using the asymptotic variances derived in our theorems, with all expectations replaced by empirical averages. Lastly, we calculated the empirical coverage of the estimated 95% confidence intervals. All R code used for the simulation studies and can be found at
https://github.com/jesusepfvazquez/right-censored-covariates
.

:
Efficiency comparison of estimators against the CC estimator under independent covariate right-censoring with known
f_{C,X|{\mathbf{Z}}}(c,x,{\mathbf{z}};\mbox{\boldmath$\alpha$})
.

Note: The red-dashed line represents no gain in efficiency compared to the CC estimator. Values below this line (
<100
%) indicate greater efficiency, while values above it (
>100

\widehat{\boldsymbol{\theta}}
across all simulations are illustrated. The Mean SE were computed using the asymptotic variances derived in our theorems, with all expectations replaced by empirical averages.
